**The marks used on this page:**

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| A footnote reading "*Role*, *date* (DEC-nn)" | The decision of that role ([governance/roles.md](../../governance/roles.md)), in the register ([§13](#13-decisions-register)), with the day it was made. The footnotes are in [Notes](#notes) |
| A footnote reading "Ratified by the *Role*, *date* (DEC-nn)" | A clarification written by the refactor lead (the agent that leads the refactor and wrote this page's first version), ratified by that role |
| A footnote reading "Proposed, for the *Role*" | Written by the refactor lead and not yet decided by that role[^dec-85]. A plan may rely on one only with that role's approval. | **OPEN (Q-nn, step N)** | Not decided. Described as the code is today, with the interim rule; the named step's brief brings it to the Solution Architect ([§14](#14-open-decisions)) |
| n/a: reason | The column does not apply, and why. A cell is never blank, and never a bare "—", "n/a" or "design". |
| today | What the code does now, with its evidence (a file, a commit, a finding). It describes and decides nothing. The "today" and "writer today" columns and all of §11 are of this kind. |
| no footnote | **Ratified at version 1.6**[^dec-85], except a sentence marked proposed or OPEN: the agreed design (03's decisions 1–30, DEC-01…DEC-30, as this page restated them at `108e7858`), and every proposal of versions 1.0–1.4, which the Solution Architect ratified one by one (DEC-87…DEC-99, DEC-105…DEC-121, DEC-123). RAT-11 was rejected. A sentence written in v1.7 is not "ratified at 1.6": it was written in v1.7 from the decision it cites, and its footnote names the DEC id. ## 1. Purpose and how to use it

This page is the source of truth for **what holds data in PinPoint Studio, who owns it, and where it lives**. It
plays the role for data that `governance/artefacts/design_system/README.md` plays for the UI.

### 1.1 When to read it

| When | What to do |
|---|---|
| Before writing any plan or brief that touches data | Read this page. Copy [§10](#10-the-ownership-questions) into the plan's "Ownership" section and answer it for every new or changed thing, citing the ids of the elements it touches[^dec-105]. |
| Before changing code that holds, derives or stores data | Find the element in [§5](#5-domain-objects)–[§8](#8-persistence) and its status in [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today). |
| When reviewing a diff | Answer the seven ownership questions for the diff, and check that §11 still matches the code the diff touches[^dec-105]. A "no" is CHANGES NEEDED. |
| When this page changes what the walkthrough deck shows | Refresh `docs/developer/information_model_walkthrough.pptx`, the developer walkthrough of this page. The Product Owner regenerates it, and its title slide names the version it reflects. The change's commit says the deck needs refreshing, and so does the dashboard while it is behind[^po24-deck]. |
| When `architecture_lint_test` fails | Fix the code. Never add a line to its allowlist to make it pass; an allowlist entry needs the Solution Architect's approval, and each entry names the refactor step that removes it. |

### 1.2 What counts as an information-model change

**HARD: any information-model change needs the Solution Architect's approval before it is built.** Propose the need, what it affects
and the answers to the ownership questions, then wait. The definition below is the one the §10 checklist and the
reviewer use, word for word:

> An information-model change (IMC, v1.1) is any edit to code, data, a file format or a plan that adds, moves,
> renames, splits, merges or removes any of the following, or changes what this page says about one: (C1) a domain
> object, an owner of facts, or what an aggregate contains; (C2) a kind of fact (a result, a measurement
> family, a golfer input), or the kind of a fact (source, recorded input, derived, recorded product, application
> state, view state) or its level (shot, session, athlete, application);
> (C3) a persisted file, a field family in one (a new block or group of keys, or a schema-version change of an
> existing file), or a settings key that holds a domain fact; (C4) the one writer of a file, or the repository it is
> written through; (C5) an identity: a new key, a new representation, a new translation to a foreign id, or
> identifying an athlete, a session or a shot by anything but its id, or adding an id type; (C6) a dependency
> between levels: a level
> reading anything but the level below as results, or context flowing down that is not a typed input; (C7) a
> domain event, its producer, or what it recomputes; (C8) an object's lifecycle: who creates or ends it, its
> states, or the commands between them; (C9) a ring, or the ring of a directory or file; (C10) anything domain- or
> session-scoped created by a view; (C11) a principle (P1…P11) or a rule (R-*). It is not an information-model
> change to make code match this page, to add
> a metric to the catalogue within an existing measurement family, to bump a rule's or a stage's version when the
> bump only makes caches stale (the stamp already carries it), to update §11, or to edit a glossary entry's wording
> when the edit cites the decision it follows. A version bump that would rewrite
> a recorded input is a change, proposed with a dry-run count of what it would rewrite. When in doubt, it is a
> change.

This definition is adopted[^dec-68], as is the rule for a version bump that would rewrite a
recorded input[^dec-84]. `CLAUDE.md`'s rule points here and restates nothing[^dec-76]. C11 (principles and rules only) and the glossary
exclusion are decided[^dec-139]; with them the definition is v1.1.
"Measurement family" is defined in §12[^dec-107].

C5's last clause is the stricter wording[^dec-106].

**A C1 or C3 change names the object's lifecycle.** A C1 or C3 change names the object's section in the lifecycle model ([`lifecycle_model.md`](lifecycle_model.md)), or adds one in the same commit[^sa25-life]. AD-51 (`governance/controls/plan.md`) applies the same rule to a plan.

| Change | Example |
|---|---|
| C1 a new first-class object or owner | a new diagnostics owner; a new aggregate |
| C3 a new persisted file, or a new field family in one | a new `<session>/*.json`; a new block in the shot's `review` |
| C5 a new identity key | identifying an athlete, a session or a shot by anything but its id; a new id type |
| C6 a new dependency between levels | the athlete level reading shot findings; a shot reading another session |
| C7 a new event | anything beyond [§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute) |
| C10 anything created by a view | a QML file instantiating a C++ type that holds session or athlete data |

### 1.3 Ids

Every element has a stable id. An id is never reused, even after its element is removed (the change log says
what it was). Cite ids in plans, briefs, reviews, commit messages and lint messages[^dec-105].

| Prefix | What | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `P1`…`P9` | Principles | [§3](#3-the-principles) |
| `R-<AREA>-n` | Rules (`REC` Recording contract, `INS` instruments, `CLUB`, `CMD` commands, `HL` headless, `APP` application state, `DER` derived facts, `ANA` the analysis, `STAMP`, `PERS` persistence, `INPUT` golfer input, `CON` concurrency, `QML`, `UNASSIGNED` the Unassigned owner, `CTX` the contexts) | §2–§9 |
| `OBJ-<NAME>` | Domain objects, application-state objects and value types | [§5.3](#53-the-objects), [§6](#6-application-state) |
| `ID-<NAME>` | Identities | [§5.2](#52-identities) |
| `EVT-nn` | Domain events | [§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute) |
| `FILE-<AREA>-nn` | Persisted files (`SHOT`, `SESS`, `ATH`, `LIB`, `APP`) | [§8.1](#81-files) |
| `SET-nn` | Settings keys that hold domain or rig facts | [§8.2](#82-settings-that-hold-domain-facts) |
| `C1`…`C11` | The kinds of information-model change | [§1.2](#12-what-counts-as-an-information-model-change) |
| `DEC-nn` | Decisions | [§13](#13-decisions-register) |
| `Q-nn` | Open decisions | [§14](#14-open-decisions) |
| `LC-<OBJ>` | Lifecycles | [§5.4](#54-lifecycles) |
| `CLS-<NAME>` | Class specifications | [§5.5](#55-what-each-class-is-for) |
| `RING-<NAME>` | Ring placements | [§4.1](#41-directories-mapped) |
| `EXT-nn` | Files that are not domain | [§8.3](#83-files-the-app-reads-or-writes-that-are-not-domain) |
| `GL-nn` | Glossary terms | [§12](#12-glossary) |
| `RAT-nn` | Proposals for the Solution Architect to ratify (none stands in v1.6) |
| `MD-nn` | The final re-audit's questions, all closed in v1.8[^dec-130-140] | [§14](#14-open-decisions) |
| `TERM-n`, `LIVE-n`, `NAME-n`, `NOTDOM-n` | Rows of §3's two terms, §5.6, §4.1's names and §6.1 | where they stand | marked where they stand |
| `M-<NAME>` | Named models | [§2.4](#24-the-shots-layers) |
| `A1`…`A9` | Lint rules (`architecture_lint_test`) | [§1.4](#14-the-lint-rules) |

### 1.4 The lint rules

The lint is `src/Gui/tests/architecture_lint_test.cpp` (03 §11, agreed; held here as canonical)[^dec-40]. The allowlist
is tagged by the migration step that removes each entry, and adding an entry needs the Solution Architect's approval.

| Rule | Catches |
|---|---|
| A1 | an include from an inner ring into an outer one ([§4](#4-the-rings-and-the-directory-mapping)); and an include from `Stores` or `Vocabulary/Recording` into `Gui` (today's hits allowlisted, each with the step that removes it)[^dec-146]. Widened in step 2, as its item S1-g[^j-1]: an Adapters-ring file outside `Gui`, or a `Vocabulary/Recording` file, never includes a `Gui` header |
| A2 | QML creating a type marked domain- or session-scoped (today: `SessionDiagnosticsModel`, `SessionSeriesModel`, `LmSessionModel`, `Charts*Presenter`, `TrustPresenter`, `SwingDataSource`) |
| A3 | folder-name parsing outside `SessionIdentity` / `SwingPaths` |
| A4 | a session or athlete file name spelled outside its owner. Today it covers FILE-SESS-02…05, FILE-ATH-01…03 and FILE-ATH-05 (eight names); the other files of §8 are not checked yet. Step 2 extends it to every FILE-SESS and FILE-ATH name, spelled only in `session_files` and `athlete_files`[^dec-160]. Since the platform refactor's C-4 it also holds FILE-APP-07, `rig.json`, spelled only in the Rig's store (`Stores/rig_store`): the design's "lint A5's table of file names" names the table A4 holds[^sa24-rig] |
| A5 | P11: file or directory access (`QFile`, `QSaveFile`, `QDir`, `QDirIterator`, `QFileInfo` on a path, `atomicWrite`, `FolderLock`, …) or an include of a repository (the repository interfaces and the stores and file doors that implement them), in any directory outside the repositories: allowed only in `Stores` (the stores and file doors), the Platform ring's own helpers, and, for a repository include, `DomainModel` (the owning objects) (file access in `DomainModel` stays a finding)[^dec-166]; the tools and the tests are not read. Widened in step 2, as its item S1-g, from "file I/O in a rules directory (Coaching, Diagnostics, Analysis rules)" (today's hits allowlisted, each with the step that removes it)[^dec-161]. The exempt set is narrower than "outside the repositories and adapters"[^dec-161]: only the stores, the Platform helpers, the tools and the tests, not every Adapters directory[^p11-lint-2]. A store door, a store's file work in a file of its own, is part of its store and exempt with it: `session_list_reads` and `session_index_summary` are the session store's, `rule_data_reads` is `RuleDataStore`'s. No file in `Stores` is counted by name[^sa24-r9] |
| A6 | a `QVariantMap`/`QVariantList` added to a presenter's or a domain type's public API |
| A7 | a new function over 300 lines in the Vocabulary and Rules rings |
| A8 | Analysis code reaching past the Recording contract into Acquisition (device classes, the ring buffer, live controllers) or into application state (the athlete controller, settings); and any Vocabulary, Rules or Domain-model file including the settings handle (`Core/pp_settings`), which is Platform since Stage B, Part 1, so A1 no longer stops it[^sa24-r1-4] |
| A9 | An include from one context into a later one (R-CTX-1), by §4.1's Context column (a **Shared** Vocabulary area is usable by every context, as Platform is[^sa24-q27]); or from a context into Domain + Application other than its contracts, a named set of the Domain model's interface and value-type headers[^sa24-q26]: a header that declares only interfaces and value types, and no owner class or service. The set is five headers: `recording_source.h` and `shot_command_values.h` (the pipeline), `recording_repository.h` (`Gui`'s capture code, once Stage D tags it Acquisition), and what they include, `recording_reader.h` and `session_input.h`; and, since C-5a.1, `rig_commands.h`, the Rig's commands as interfaces (the Rig design addendum's decision 1[^sa25-add1]), six in all; any other header joins the set by a ruling, when a context first needs it[^sa24-r13]. A **Shared** Vocabulary file never includes a context's file, as Platform never depends outward (Q27)[^cm24]. A C++ file whose directory has no context fails, so a new directory is placed on purpose. Built in the platform refactor's Stage B (B1.21), with no allowlist line[^ctx-24] |
| A10 | an include of the Rig's header, `DomainModel/rig.h`, from any file but the three kinds that hold the Rig: the composition root (`Gui/main`, `App/`), `app.rig` (`Gui/app/rig_presenter`) and the Rig's store (`Stores/rig_store`); `rig.cpp` is its own. A manager reaches the Rig through its contracts, never this header (rig_design.md §6 rule 8). Built in the platform refactor's C-4, with no allowlist line[^sa24-rig] |

A change to a lint rule's scope, or to A4's list, is an information-model change (C4, C9)[^dec-120].

## 2. The four contexts and their contracts

PinPoint is four contexts: **Acquisition → Diagnostics → Coaching**, joined by contracts (the Recording, §2.2, and
the results, below), and **Domain + Application**, the hub each works through by its contracts[^ctx-24][^sa24-q26].
Data flows left to right; along the chain, code dependencies point right to left (R-CTX-1). Context flows back from Domain + Application as typed input. Diagnostics is analysis and diagnosis: the
context this page called Analysis before v1.10.0[^ctx-24].

```
 ┌──────────────┐ Recording ┌───────────────────┐ results ┌───────────────────┐ plans,  ┌──────────────────────────┐
 │ ACQUISITION  │ ────────▶ │ DIAGNOSTICS       │ ──────▶ │ COACHING          │ ──────▶ │ DOMAIN + APPLICATION     │
 │ transports,  │           │ analysis graph,   │ (find-  │ what to work on,  │ evidence│ Athlete · Session · Shot │
 │ devices,     │           │ firing findings,  │  ings,  │ how, how much to  │         │ · Studio/Rig · the       │
 │ ring buffer, │           │ cross-shot        │  diag-  │ tell, whether it  │ ◀────── │ coaching objects ·       │
 │ connectors,  │           │ reductions and    │  nosis) │ stuck: the work-  │ context │ persistence · AppState · │
 │ calibration  │           │ the derived facts │         │ on lifecycle, the │         │ presentation             │
 │ procedures   │           │                   │         │ feedback policies │         │ owns and stores          │
 └──────────────┘           └───────────────────┘         └───────────────────┘         │ everything               │
                                                                                         └──────────────────────────┘
        Platform (§4, ring 0) is the base every context uses. It is not a context: it holds nothing domain-shaped.
```

| Context | Holds | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| **Acquisition** | Transports, devices, the ring buffer, connectors, calibration *procedures*, threading inside capture, and the signal-level sensor maths: the IMU's orientation and calibration maths (`imu_calibration.h`, `trunk_angles.h`, `trunk_stand_tall.h`; the full list: §4.1, RING-RULES)[^ctx-24]. It produces the Recording. Its internals are designed in the platform refactor (capture), which follows this one; only its contract is fixed here. | Nothing persistent: it produces Recordings, which the domain owns and stores |
| **Diagnostics** | The analysis graph (per shot), with the golf-specific fusion and kinematics; the findings rule over the diagnostic graph; the derived facts (outcomes, factors, tendencies, priorities); the session's and the athlete's diagnosis rules (the two graphs' names)[^dec-132][^ctx-24] | Nothing persistent: its results are owned and stored by the domain |
| **Coaching** | What the golfer works on, how, how much they are told, and whether it stuck: the work-on lifecycle and the feedback policies (`docs/design/coaching_layer.md`). It has no rules yet: its objects, files, events and rules are information-model changes made in the coaching track[^ctx-24] | Nothing persistent (below) |
| **Domain + Application** | Athlete, Session, Shot, Studio/Rig, the coaching objects, persistence, `RuleData`, `AppState`, presentation. It hands the other contexts their context as typed input: athlete facts, rig state, session usuals, norms and baselines | Everything that is stored |

**Coaching owns nothing persistent**, like Acquisition and Diagnostics[^ctx-24]. Its stored objects are domain
objects, owned at the athlete and session levels and keyed by the owners' ids: the golfer model, the work-on
lifecycle's state, the playbook and the practice log. Each is an information-model change of its own (C1, C3), made
in the coaching track. Facts about one shot or one session stay on their one owner, as a part with its own writer, as
the shot's `review` and `derived` blocks are today.

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-CTX-1 | **Data flows left to right; code dependencies point right to left**[^ctx-24]. The order is Acquisition → Diagnostics → Coaching. **Domain + Application is not ordered after them in code: it is the hub every context works through.** A context reaches it through its **contracts**: the Domain model's interfaces and value types (the repository interfaces, `RecordingSource`, the commands' value types, the result values). It never reaches it through the owner classes (`Shot`, `Session`, `Athlete`) or the Application services; Domain + Application may use every context, since it composes them[^sa24-q26]. Within the order, a later context may depend on an earlier context's contracts and rules; an earlier context never depends on a later one. Coaching reads Diagnostics' results and never re-derives them; Diagnostics knows nothing of coaching; Acquisition knows nothing of either. Context flows back only as typed input from Domain + Application. A context that must learn of something later in the chain subscribes to the domain's events ([§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute)); it never names a later context. A coaching modality (lesson, practice, play, fire-fighting, review) changes only feedback, Coaching's own reductions and what is shown (`coaching_layer.md` T5: analysis is session-agnostic). Checked by lint A9 from Stage B of the platform refactor ([§1.4](#14-the-lint-rules)) | An earlier context including or naming a later one; Coaching re-deriving a Diagnostics result; **acquisition or diagnostics code branching on a coaching modality** |

**The results contract**[^ctx-24]. Diagnostics → Coaching is a contract, as the Recording (§2.2) is for Acquisition →
Diagnostics. Coaching reads the owners' result values, through the Domain model's contracts (R-CTX-1), never the owner classes themselves:
- a shot's derived data and findings (OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED, OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS);
- the session's diagnosis and summary (OBJ-SESSION-DIAG, OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY);
- the session's series of measurements (OBJ-SESSION-SERIES);
- the athlete's diagnosis (OBJ-ATHLETE-DIAG).

It never reads raw streams and never runs an analysis stage.

**Where Coaching starts**[^ctx-24]. The session's declared focus, the declared work-on (OBJ-SESSION-INTENT,
FILE-SESS-02; R-INPUT-3; GL-68), is Coaching's starting point. The declared miss and the screen answers in the same
object stay Diagnostics' inputs. `work_ons.json` (FILE-SESS-05) is **not** coaching state, despite its name: it holds
the session summary (OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY), a Diagnostics product.

**The packs**[^ctx-24]. Diagnostics' code sits in four folders by layer, lowest first: `src/Diagnostics/model` (the
vocabulary, the characteristic, the norm, the context, pack reading), `content` (the packs and norm packs, their loaders,
validators, registries and provider interfaces), `derived` (the derived facts) and `engine` (detection, explanation,
the measure sources, the health check), which fires findings over everything below it. A file includes only its own
folder and those before it, checked by the test `diagnostics_dir_order_check`[^sa26-r8]. One loader, `RuleData`
(R-CON-4's immutable snapshots, in Domain + Application), loads the packs for every context.
Screens (`src/Diagnostics/content/screen_pack.*`) stay in Diagnostics: a screen answer is an input to diagnosis
(R-INPUT-3), saying whether a fault is a capacity limit. Drills (`src/Diagnostics/content/drill_pack.*`)
are Coaching's intervention library. Today a diagnostics condition names its drill, and the health check
(`src/Diagnostics/engine/diagnostics_health.*`) checks that the drill exists: a Diagnostics rule naming a Coaching fact,
which R-CTX-1 forbids. So the link from a condition to its drill moves to Coaching, and the cross-pack health check
moves to `RuleData`'s level, where both packs are visible. Diagnostics says what is wrong; Coaching says what to do about
it. The packs' format change needs the Solution Architect's approval, and the pack editor's change the Design Lead's,
before either is built. The drill pack stays tagged Diagnostics until no Diagnostics file includes it: today the health
check (`diagnostics_health.*`), the file pack provider (`file_pack_provider.cpp`) and the swing themes pack
(`swing_themes_pack.h`) do. So lint A9 has no allowlist line for it.

**Intent and cold tests at the boundary**[^ctx-24].
- **Intent.** A golfer's stated intent is a golfer fact, owned by the domain (R-INPUT-1, R-INPUT-2). Diagnostics may
  summarise it (R-INPUT-4 and R-INPUT-5, the intent-consistency summaries), but it never excuses, suppresses or
  re-grades a finding because of it. Judging a shot against its stated intent (the play debrief, decision review) is
  Coaching's.
- **Cold tests.** A cold retest is a labelled Coaching test over shots that still count in full in every Diagnostics
  reduction. There are no warm-up shots.

**Contexts and rings are different cuts**[^dec-48]. A context describes how data
flows; a ring ([§4](#4-the-rings-and-the-directory-mapping)) describes which code may depend on which. So one
context spans several rings:

| Example | Context | Ring |
|---|---|---|
| A reduction *rule* (tendencies, the flight check) | Diagnostics | Rules |
| The findings rule, `fireFindings()` | Diagnostics | Rules |
| The *owner* that runs them and keeps their results (`SessionDiagnostics`, `AthleteDiagnostics`) | Domain + Application | Domain model |
| The analysis pipeline that runs the analysis graph | Diagnostics | Application |

### 2.1 The shapes of Diagnostics

**Firing findings** is the name in the target for a shot checking each of its numbers against its corridor; **diagnosis**
is reserved for the session's work over its shots' findings[^dec-100]. The code calls the shot's part `detectShot` (a
member of `SessionDiagnosticsModel`); step 3 makes it a pure function and renames it (e.g. `fireFindings`).

| Shape | What it is | Run by |
|---|---|---|
| **The analysis graph** (per shot)[^dec-132] | A pure function: Recording + effective context → the shot's analysis (the recorded product). A dependency graph of versioned stages, not a stack (`analysis_dag_design.md`). | The analysis pipeline (an application service). **It never fires findings.**[^dec-19][^dec-100] |
| **Firing findings** | A pure function, `fireFindings()` (step 3's name) in `src/Diagnostics`: the shot's analysis and the context its `Session` passes down (club baseline, norms, effective club) → the shot's findings[^dec-100], which the `Shot` posts, stamped, to its `Session`, which keeps them[^dec-135]. | Each `Shot` fires its own findings; on a corridor or norm edit the `Session` asks each shot to re-fire, with no re-analysis[^dec-100] |
| **Cross-shot reductions** | The session's usuals (GL-25)[^dec-130]; the session's diagnosis from its shots' findings (tiers, patterns, links, reasons, per-club mixes, the summary)[^dec-100], athlete diagnosis, the derived facts ([§7](#7-derived-facts)) (the cross-shot "assessment" of 03 is "diagnosis")[^dec-125]. The shot's outcome, its three factors, its carry and its flight-check overlay are not reductions: they are the shot's derived data, computed for the shot with the session's usuals passed down, which feed the assessment that fires its findings (M-SHOT-LAYERS)[^dec-121]. Incremental and stateful over many shots, recomputed by events. | `SessionDiagnostics`, `AthleteDiagnostics` |

### 2.2 The Recording contract

A **Recording** (OBJ-RECORDING) is everything Diagnostics is allowed to know about a shot, and nothing else[^dec-16].

| Part | Contents |
|---|---|
| Kind | Which kind of shot it is: **captured** (it has streams) or **device-only** (a launch monitor alone saw it: no streams). An LM-only shot **is** a Recording[^dec-44]. |
| Streams | Video frames, IMU and HackMotion samples, the launch monitor's readings as delivered. A device-only Recording has only the reading. |
| Timestamps | On one stated clock model: camera timestamps mark exposure end; Spinnaker frames are host-stamped (about ±0.4 ms jitter) |
| Devices | Device identity and configuration |
| Capture facts | The session type the shot was captured under (`capture.sessionType`), recorded like its analysis context; the session type itself is the `Session`'s context, kept in the session file (FILE-SESS-01). Also the shot source, the impact time and the capture settings (today: `src/Vocabulary/Recording/recording.h`). The recorded type is read so old files load, and decides nothing: which stages run, and what each produces, follows only the data and devices the shot has[^dec-202]. Until #273 retires its steering, the job builder still reads it to choose a stage list (today). SwingLab may override it for a run (`sessionTypeOverride`); the app never does; the input stamp names it[^dec-88]. |
| Rig | A reference to the Studio/Rig state it was captured with (cameras, positions, calibrations) |
| Integrity | Capture holes, lost frames |
| Analysis context | The athlete facts the analysis consumes (handedness, height, club set, the club and its length), **recorded at capture**, so re-analysis never reads the current athlete record. It is the only source of athlete facts for analysis[^dec-49]. A recorded input (P1). |
| Analysis context on disk | One schema-tagged `analysisContext` block in the swing file, written once by capture through the one header writer: `athlete.handedness`, `athlete.heightM` (0 = unknown), `athlete.clubSet` (men / women / junior), the captured club (`club.name`, empty = undeclared, never the stub) with its geometry, length prior and standard length, and `bag`, a snapshot of the bag's geometry so a corrected club is resolvable without the current athlete record. `capture.club` stays, written from the same value, as provenance[^dec-24][^dec-32][^dec-33]. The norm cohort (sex, age band) is **not** recorded in step 1[^dec-30]; firing findings takes it from the athlete record on the shot's date, not from the Recording, and records it in the stamp [^dec-171]. |

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-REC-1 | A shot's swing file is written **once by capture** (the Recording), **then only by the `Shot` itself, through the repository**[^dec-86]. The `Shot` makes the save[^dec-103]. The only other writes are one-off migrations (R-PERS-7) that the Solution Architect approves one by one, such as the step-1 backfill of height and club set[^dec-34][^dec-108]. | Any other writer of a swing file |
| R-REC-2 | **Capture writes in stages**: the shot (`RecordingRepository::create`, which `Session::addShot` calls)[^dec-142], then its late parts through `RecordingRepository::completeCapture`. The late parts are a closed list: (a) a late launch-monitor reading, which may carry the club the device named: follow-club (SET-08) stores that **device club** beside the captured club, as its own source fact, and never overwrites the captured club or the shot's `review`; the effective-club rule prefers the device club when follow-club is on, so the analysis comes out as it did under today's interim[^dec-133][^dec-82]. The device club is stored as `capture.deviceClub`, in `capture.club`'s shape; the interim overwrite of the captured club and of a seeded `review.club` is gone (step 6)[^built-6-2]. A device club changes the effective context, so the analysis is stale by its input stamp (R-ANA-5); (b) a phone clip's stream origin and, once its bytes land, its file, frame times and rate[^dec-140]. A new kind of late part is an IMC (C3, C4)[^dec-27]. | A late write that is not one of these |
| R-REC-3 | **Until step 3** the later writers are today's callers, all through `RecordingRepository`: `ShotListModel`, `ReanalysisController` and the one write-back `reanalyseAndPersist`, the launch monitor controller, `PpcpClipFiler`, `SessionReviewController` (the summary sidecar), `SessionArchiver` (its stub, through the public `SwingStore::save`, today), `LibraryConverter` and the tools (`lm_repair`, `pps_convert_library`, `swinglab_run`). The `Shot` takes them over in step 3[^dec-86]. | Writing the swing file past the repository |
| R-REC-4 | **Analysis only ever runs on a Recording**, whether it arrived a second ago or a year ago. Live analysis and re-analysis are the same function: **same Recording + same effective context (the effective club included) → same job.** | Building an analysis job from live objects; Analysis code reaching into device classes, the ring buffer, live controllers, the athlete controller or settings (lint A8) |
| R-REC-5 | **One builder** makes the analysis job from the Recording and its effective context (the recorded analysis context, with the golfer's club correction applied), for live capture and re-analysis alike. | A second job builder; any context field set on one path only |
| R-REC-6 | **Re-analysis uses the effective club**[^dec-18]: when the golfer has corrected the club, the builder puts the corrected club, its length and its norms into the effective context, because it is what was really hit. A corrected shot re-analyses with its corrected club. The captured club stays in the Recording for provenance. After a correction the analysis is **stale only**: it is re-analysed when the golfer asks, or by the library sweep[^dec-39]. | Re-analysing a corrected shot with the club it was captured under; Diagnostics reading the shot's `review` block itself |
| R-REC-7 | **The club rule** is R-CLUB-2; R-CLUB-3, the rule until step 6[^dec-26][^dec-31], is retired[^built-6-2]. | Treating the popover's club, or the board's evidence suggestion, as the correction |
| R-REC-8 | **A device-only Recording**[^dec-44] has no streams, a launch-monitor reading, the measurement rows derived from it and one Impact phase. The analysis graph has nothing to run on; re-analysing it derives the rows again from the recorded reading (`writeAnalysis` with no analysis). The kind is the `Shot`'s own fact: the one header writer writes it when the shot is created, and a document without it is classified once, in the repository's reader (`isDeviceOnly` today)[^dec-87]. Its key is the top-level `shotKind`, `captured` or `deviceOnly`; old files are not backfilled and are classified by today's content rule[^dec-147]. The kinds are an open list, and the rig reference is never derived from the kind[^sa24-rig] (the rig reference: built by C-8a, C-8b). | Treating a device-only shot as a separate store or a separate model |

**The name.** The contract's types are vocabulary shared by both sides; its store is an adapter. The types live in
`src/Vocabulary/Recording`[^dec-29]; the store is in `src/Stores`, the stores' folder[^sa24-r6].

**In the code** (status: partly, [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today)):

| Element | Where | Ring |
|---|---|---|
| `Recording` (window and `t0Us`, sources by role with the one perspective mapping, capture facts, ball inputs, IMU bindings, integrity inputs; every time window-relative) | `src/Vocabulary/Recording/recording.h` | Vocabulary |
| `AnalysisContext` (handedness, height, club set, the captured club with its geometry, length prior and standard length, the bag snapshot) | `src/Vocabulary/Recording/analysis_context.h` | Vocabulary |
| `effectiveContext()` v2, R-CLUB-2; its inputs are the recorded context, the shot's two club edits and its device club (`ClubReview`: the board's override, the popover's club, `capture.deviceClub`)[^built-6-2] | `src/Analysis/effective_context.*` | Rules |
| `buildAnalysisJob()`, the one builder; `ShotAnalysisJob` | `src/Analysis/analysis_job_builder.*`, `shot_analysis_job.h` | Rules |
| The one header writer and the `analysisContext` block's writer and reader | `src/Stores/recording_header.*` | Adapters (the store) |
| The Recording loader (a swing folder → the Recording + the review facts) | `src/Stores/recording_loader.*` | Adapters (the store) |
| `RecordingRepository`; its adapter `SwingStoreRecordingRepository` | `src/DomainModel/recording_repository.h`; `src/Stores/swing_store_repository.*` | Domain model; Adapters |
| `RecordingReader` (a swing folder → the Recording + the review facts; separate from the write door, so frame decoding stays out of the review writers); its adapter `SwingStoreRecordingReader` (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/DomainModel/recording_reader.h`; `src/Stores/recording_loader.*` | Domain model; Adapters |
| `AnalysisService` (the run, and the Recording's integrity verdicts); `integrityFor()` (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/Analysis/analysis_service.*`; `src/Analysis/recording_integrity.*` | Application; Rules |
| The capture-side snapshot (the current facts → the Recording, read once at the window freeze) (step 1.5, `d07ccebb`) | `src/Gui/shot/capture_context.*` | Adapters |

### 2.3 Studio/Rig

**OBJ-RIG. The Rig: one per machine, at the application level.**[^dec-17][^sa24-rig]

Built so far (C-4, C-5a.1): the class (`src/DomainModel/rig.*`), with its camera commands (`rig_commands.h`), called by nothing in the app yet; its repository (`rig_repository.h`); its store
(`src/Stores/rig_store.*`) and file (FILE-APP-07); and `app.rig`, read-only. The rig facts still live where the last row says.
The platform refactor's Stage C builds the rest item by item; each row below names the item that builds it.

| Aspect | Target |
|---|---|
| What it is | `Rig`, a Domain-model entity (`src/DomainModel/rig.*`), one per machine: each machine holds its own. It is the camera calibration design's "Bay", the persistent, named physical set-up a session attaches to, and that design's per-bay store is the Rig's store (FILE-APP-07)[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-4 to C-6b.2 |
| Its two parts | **The rig state** (versioned): per camera, by its `DeviceKey` (ID-DEVICE-KEY), the perspective, mirrored, fixed in place, the hitting-area box and its calibration records; per sensor placement, by its `PlacementKey` (ID-PLACEMENT-KEY), the role and the alignment record held for the run. A change makes a new rig state and emits EVT-15. **The Rig's other facts** (not versioned): the display aliases; whether each device is enabled on this machine (a machine-wide preference that seeds each session, not what a shot was captured with); the learned club-length prior (SET-05)[^dec-153]; and `ppcp/studioName`, the Rig's label, which Stage F moves. A change to these is saved, and makes no new state and no `RigChanged`; a change to an alias or an enabled flag emits `RigDeviceLabelChanged` (EVT-24)[^sa24-rig][^sa25-add8] |
| Not owned by | A device, or a shot |
| Relation to shots | Each Recording carries the rig reference it was captured with: an embedded copy of the rig state, with its `RigRef` (ID-RIG; FILE-SHOT-01's `rig` block). A Recording's rig is read from the Recording, never from the current Rig (R-RIG-2)[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-8a, C-8b |
| Calibration | The *procedure* belongs to Acquisition; its *result* is a source fact owned by the Rig: a calibration record (`CalibrationRecord`: subject, kind, method, parameters, uncertainty, the time observed, and optionally where its evidence is kept) in the rig state. A step machine returns its record and an adapter hands it to the Rig (R-RIG-4). A worn sensor's alignment is held for the run of the app: it is never saved to FILE-APP-07 or re-applied at launch, and every path that clears a sensor's calibration clears the Rig's record (R-RIG-3). "Fixed cameras are square" and the uncalibrated down-the-line yaw σ are recorded as `extrinsics` records with method `assumed`[^sa24-rig]; the analysis reads the down-the-line yaw σ (9° uncalibrated, 1° calibrated) from the camera assumptions' home, `src/Vocabulary/Rig/camera_assumptions.h`, and its values and output are unchanged[^sa27-c7b-r4]. A face-on camera fixed in place is square and level: yaw, pitch and roll 0, with no orientation uncertainty (AD-7); a down-the-line camera with no measured direction, fixed or not, is level along the target line, its yaw uncertain by 9° and no uncertainty claimed for pitch or roll; any other camera, a face-on camera not marked fixed among them, has none[^sa27-c7b]. The Rig derives each from the camera's set-up at every command that changes it and at launch, where a state changed by the derivation is saved once, with no event[^sa27-c7b]. What each record says is in the same header[^sa27-c7b][^sa27-c7b-r4] (proposed, for the Solution Architect: a measured `extrinsics` record replacing the assumed one, and no time observed on an assumed record, C-7b). Built by C-3 (the record), C-6b.2 (today's set and clear paths), C-7b (the assumed records), C-11a to C-13b (the procedures) |
| One writer | Only `Rig` changes either part, by its own commands, and only `Rig` saves, through `RigRepository` (an interface in the Domain model; its adapter in the stores folder). Today's writers call its commands instead: the C++ writers (`CameraManager`, `CameraInstance`, `ImuManager`, `ShotProcessor`) through `RigCommands` (`rig_commands.h`, among the Domain model's contracts), which `Rig` implements and the composition root hands to each; QML through `app.rig`[^sa24-rig][^sa25-add1]. Built by C-4; the writers cut over in C-5b, C-6b.1 and C-7 |
| Commands | `assignPerspective`, `setMirrored`, `setFixedInPlace`, `setHittingArea`, `clearHittingArea`, `claimRole`, `releaseRole`, `commitCalibration`, `clearCalibration`; for the unversioned part, `setAlias`, `setEnabledOnThisMachine`, `learnClubLength`. There is no `setPlacement` (the placement is derived from the roles) and no `setMountOrientation` (unused). A hitting-area or perspective edit is one command at the end of the edit[^sa24-rig]. The lifecycle: LC-RIG. Built by C-5a.1 (camera commands), C-6a.2 (roles; sensor alias and enablement), C-6b.2 (`commitCalibration`, `clearCalibration`), C-7 (`learnClubLength`) |
| Invariants | **Impact is exclusive**: assigning it to one camera clears it from every other. **One claim per role**: a second claim of a role is refused or displaces as `imu_roles::claimRole` decides today, with the hardware facts handed to it as a typed value; the Rig never asks a device. Stage C adds no other; the camera list has no fixed length[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-5a.1 (Impact), C-6a.2 (roles) |
| Identity, persistence, `RigChanged` | ID-RIG; FILE-APP-07; EVT-15[^sa24-rig]. Each Recording keeps each stream's setup (perspective, mirrored, fixed in place), which the job builder reads; from C-8b the `rig` block is written beside it, from the same values, and analysis does not read the block |
| How a view reaches it | `app.rig`, a presenter in the Adapters ring that exposes typed gadgets and list models (cameras, sensors, and the derived placement view that `imuPlacement` is today) and forwards commands. No view reads the Rig's file or the settings keys it replaced. Only the composition root, `app.rig` and the Rig's store include `rig.h`[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-4 (read-only), C-5b, C-6b.1 |
| Thread and failure | The Rig lives on the owner thread, the GUI thread in the app; its commands and its atomic save run there. A failed save is logged with `ppWarn`, the state in memory stands, and the next command saves again; a command is never refused because the disk failed[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-4 |
| Where rig facts live today | Settings SET-09 (the sensor and `ppcp` keys), the trunk ceremony's results (FILE-SESS-11) and the alignment on the sensors' live objects. The two assumptions in code ("fixed cameras are square", the uncalibrated DTL yaw σ) are stated records in the rig state since C-7b, saved in FILE-APP-07 (the "Calibration" row); the analysis reads the down-the-line yaw σ from the camera assumptions' home[^sa27-c7b-r4]. The other places move to the Rig one group at a time, each in the item that makes the Rig its writer (SET-05 and SET-09 name the items): the camera keys of SET-09 moved in C-5b and the learned club-length prior (SET-05[^dec-153]) in C-7, each now in FILE-APP-07. The design's places for rig facts are FILE-APP-07 and, as a projection, each Recording's `rig` block (FILE-SHOT-01); another place is an information-model change (C3)[^dec-140][^sa24-rig]. The studio profile (FILE-APP-06, SET-20) holds no rig fact: it is OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE[^dec-193]. |

| Id | Rule | Built by |
|---|---|---|
| R-RIG-1 | A rig state is never altered; a change makes a new one; an equal state id means the same set-up, and an unequal one need not mean a different set-up for a given shot[^sa24-rig]. | C-3 (the digest), C-5a.1 |
| R-RIG-2 | A Recording's rig is read from the Recording, never from the current Rig[^sa24-rig]. | C-8a |
| R-RIG-3 | Only the Rig's commands change a rig fact, and every clear of a calibration goes through the Rig[^sa24-rig]. | C-5b, C-6b.1, C-6b.2, C-7 |
| R-RIG-4 | A step machine returns its record; only an adapter calls the sink[^sa24-rig]. | C-6b.2 (the sink), C-11a to C-13b |
| R-RIG-5 | A Recording's block lists exactly the rig devices that have a stream or a binding in that shot; no enablement is consulted[^sa24-rig]. | C-8b |

### 2.4 The shot's layers

**M-SHOT-LAYERS**[^dec-121]: "The shot contains information that was acquired and collected, then processed and
analysed. That data is then analysed against the DAG to fire findings. The shot outcome and tendencies are part of
the shot derived data. They feed the assessment that fires findings." In the quote, "the DAG" is the diagnostic
graph[^dec-132][^dec-140].

| Layer | Is | Produced by | Owner | Persisted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Acquired | What was acquired and collected: the Recording (§2.2) | capture | `Shot` | FILE-SHOT-01, 03, 04, 06 |
| 2 Processed and analysed | The analysis, the recorded product (§7.2) | the analysis pipeline | `Shot` | FILE-SHOT-01 |
| 3 Shot derived data | The shot outcome; the shot's tendencies, its three factors (strike · direction · flight); carry against the usual; the flight-check overlay[^dec-122]. Computed for the shot with the session's usuals (GL-25) passed down as a typed input (P6); a change of a club's usuals makes every same-club shot recompute its derived data and re-fire its findings[^dec-130] | the shot rules (the derived facts, in Diagnostics: `src/Diagnostics/derived/`, since Stage B of the platform refactor)[^ctx-24] | computed by the `Shot` (P10; OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED), which posts it, stamped, to its `Session`; kept by the `Session`[^dec-135] | FILE-SESS-03, with the shot's findings[^dec-135] |
| 4 Assessment | The analysis and the derived data checked against the diagnostic graph (the diagnostic engine, pack + norms)[^dec-132][^dec-140], with the club baseline, norms and effective club the `Session` passes down | `fireFindings()`[^dec-100] | `Shot` | n/a: a step, not stored |
| 5 Findings | The shot's findings, 1:1 with the shot that fired them[^dec-101] | the assessment | fired by the `Shot`, which posts them, stamped, to its `Session`; kept by the `Session` as its collection (OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS)[^dec-135] | FILE-SESS-03[^dec-135] |

The outcome, the factors, the carry and the flight check are **not** findings: they feed the assessment[^dec-121]. The
`Session`'s diagnostics then diagnose from its shots' findings[^dec-100]: "the shot does not use the findings and the
session does"[^dec-135]. "The shot's tendencies" (its factors) are
not the athlete level's Tendencies (§12, GL-33, GL-64). `thumb.jpg` (FILE-SHOT-05), the phase grid and
`swing_summary.json` are caches and are not placed in a layer.

---

## 3. The principles

| # | Principle | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Source facts, recorded inputs and derived facts are different kinds of thing** (recorded input)[^dec-21]. A **source fact** is something only the world or the golfer can tell us (every instrument's readings and streams, the athlete record, what the golfer said, the history of what the golfer corrected); it is stored once, by its owner, and never regenerated. A **recorded input** is context written once at first need so a result is reproducible (the Recording's analysis context; a session's club baseline); it is never regenerated and never deleted with the caches. It is rewritten only when the rule that produced it has a newer version, and each rewrite is recorded with what it read[^dec-46]; such a rewrite needs the Solution Architect's approval first, proposed with a dry-run count[^dec-84]. The captured club is a source fact and is never overwritten: follow-club stores the device's club beside it, as its own source fact[^dec-133]; today's overwrite is a known interim until step 6 ([§11.1](#111-status)). A **derived fact** is a pure function of source facts, recorded inputs and versioned rules; it may be cached, deleted and rebuilt at any time. | Golfer input or a recorded input stored in, or deleted with, a regenerable cache; a cache holding the only copy of anything; a derived fact stored in a source record[^dec-89]; a higher level's verdict written into a lower level's facts (a shot's findings) |
| P2 | **Domain, application state and view state are three layers**, each referring only downwards. Domain is what is true without a screen (the same in a headless sweep on another machine); application state is what the app is doing now; view state is one view's scroll, tab or expanded row. (A *layer* is one of these three; a *ring* is a dependency ring, §4.)[^dec-118] | Diagnostics owned by a view; the selection held in several places; domain rules in QML |
| P3 | **Aggregates have one root; each file has exactly one writer, inside its aggregate**[^dec-42]. `Athlete` and `Session` are roots. Source facts change only through their owner's commands: an aggregate root's, or, for an entity's own facts, the entity's, reached through its root[^dec-104]. So inside the `Session` aggregate each `Shot` changes its own facts by its own commands (P10). The derived owners inside an aggregate (`SessionDiagnostics` in a `Session`, `AthleteDiagnostics` in an `Athlete`) write their own caches. The swing file's writers, capture, the `Shot` and approved migrations, are R-REC-1's. | Two objects writing one file; a source fact changed except by its owner's command; a service writing a session or athlete file except through its aggregate; changing the athlete under a running session; a view mutating findings |
| P4 | **Persistence lives behind repositories.** The domain model defines the repository interfaces (`RecordingRepository`, `SessionRepository`, `AthleteRepository`); adapters implement them[^dec-50]. One repository per file family, one format definition, schema checked on read. | File I/O in domain objects or in Coaching/Diagnostics rules; a file's envelope parsed in more than one place; a file name spelled outside its repository |
| P5 | **Derivation is an explicit dependency graph, updated by events.** Each derived fact declares its inputs and rule version; only what depends on a change is recomputed; a rule's version is part of its result's stamp. | `mutable` dirty flags that rebuild everything; stamps that omit inputs; staleness found by accident |
| P6 | **One rule, one result, one owner, at the right level.** A level reads the level below as results. Context flowing down (session usuals into a shot; the athlete baseline into a session) is a typed input. Nothing recomputes another level's facts. | Recomputing another level's facts; two rules for one fact (two direction classifiers); a gloss applied per surface |
| P7 | **Identities are types, not strings.** `AthleteId`, `SessionId`, `ShotRef` are value types ([§5.2](#52-identities)); folders, display numbers and labels are properties. | Keying by name or folder; parsing a folder name outside `SessionIdentity` / `SwingPaths` (lint A3); "Shot N" meaning anything but the swing number |
| P8 | **Work runs on immutable snapshots; results come back to the owner.** Domain objects live on one owner thread: the GUI thread in the app, the main thread in a tool[^dec-51]. Workers read immutable inputs and post results to the owning root. | A worker reading a live object; a lazy singleton first built on a worker; a new threading idiom |
| P9 | **The QML boundary is typed.** QML sees `Q_GADGET` values and `QAbstractListModel`s with declared roles. | A new `QVariantMap`/`QVariantList` in a presenter's or domain type's public API (lint A6); a domain or session-scoped object created in QML (lint A2) |
| P10 | **A `Session` is a collection of shots and their findings, plus the context they were taken in; everything specific to one shot is owned and managed by the `Shot`**[^dec-86][^dec-101]. A shot's findings have a 1:1 relationship with the shot that fired them[^dec-101]. The `Shot` is its own object, with its own commands for its own facts, and is reachable only through its `Session`. The `Session` keeps the collection (adding and trashing shots, swing numbers, order), each shot's findings and derived data, which the `Shot` fires and computes and posts to it, stamped[^dec-135], the context the shots were taken in (identity, type, status, intent run, session intent, club baseline) and the session-wide results (the summary, the series). It hears every change to a shot through the shot's events, synchronously and in order[^dec-69]. Each `Shot` fires its own findings from its analysis and the context its `Session` passes down (club baseline, norms, effective club); the `Session`'s diagnostics diagnose from those findings[^dec-100]. | A shot-specific fact, command, file write or event owned by the `Session`, except the findings and derived data its shots post to it[^dec-135]; a `Shot` reached except through its `Session` |
| P11 | **An object's persistence is its own concern**[^dec-161]. Only the owning object, through its repository, reads or writes its files. Views, rules and other objects reach its facts through the object, never its files or its repository. The one sanctioned crossing: a shot's findings and derived data, which the `Shot` computes and its `Session` keeps[^dec-135]. | A view, a rule or another object reading or writing an object's files, or including its repository (lint A5, widened in step 2 to every directory outside the repositories)[^dec-161] |

**Two terms side by side:**

| Id | Term | Is | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| TERM-1 | Recorded input | Context frozen once, so a result can be reproduced | the Recording's analysis context; `club_baseline.json` |
| TERM-2 | Recorded product | A derived fact that is expensive, so it is kept with a stamp and refreshed when stale | a shot's analysis |

---

## 4. The rings and the directory mapping

Dependencies point **inward only**: a ring may use the rings inside it, never one outside (lint A1).

```
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ ADAPTERS  Devices: cameras · IMUs · HackMotion · launch monitors (Premier, GC Quad…)  │
 │           Persistence: the Recording store · repositories   UI: presenters · QML      │
 │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
 │  │ APPLICATION  AppState · use cases (start/end session, correct, exclude, set      │  │
 │  │              intent) · services (capture, analysis pipeline, re-analysis,        │  │
 │  │              athlete catch-up, archive, report)                                  │  │
 │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │
 │  │  │ DOMAIN MODEL  Athlete · Session · Shot · a shot's measurement set ·        │  │  │
 │  │  │               diagnostics owners · repository interfaces                   │  │  │
 │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │ RULES (pure)  measurement algorithms (raw device data → measurements,│  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │   incl. the LM's inferred reads) · instrument precedence · findings · │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │   diagnostics engine + packs/norms · derived facts                   │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │ VOCABULARY  value types (Club, Intent, Measurement, …) · ids  │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │   · metric definitions + catalogue · club vocabulary · units  │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  │ PLATFORM  logging · profiling                       │      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  │   · machine queries · the worker pool               │      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │  │  │
 │  │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │  │
 │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │
 │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

A **value type** (`Club`, `Intent`, one `Measurement`) is Vocabulary; the **objects that hold them** (a shot's one set
of measurements, the session's intent) are Domain model. 03 §2 draws `Intent` and `Club` in the Domain model ring;
this page is canonical[^dec-40]: the types are Vocabulary.

| Ring | Holds | Never |
|---|---|---|
| **Platform** (ring 0)[^dec-23] | Logging (`ppInfo`/`ppWarn`), profiling, machine queries; from step 3, the one worker pool, a serial lane per session and the post-to-owner helper (R-CON-5)[^dec-169]. Every ring may include it. | Depends on anything outside itself; holds anything domain-shaped |
| **Vocabulary** | What things are: value types (`Club`, `Intent`, `Measurement`, …), identity types, metric definitions and the catalogue, club vocabulary, units, the Recording contract's types | Depends on anything but Platform |
| **Rules** | Pure functions: measurement algorithms (pose, club, ball, IMU fusion, kinematics, wrist, 3-D skeleton, the LM's inferred reads); the one instrument-precedence rule; firing findings; derived facts (outcomes, factors, flight check, Sky, tendencies, link grading)[^ctx-24] | File I/O (lint A5); golfer-facing sentences; knowing about sessions or screens |
| **Domain model** | Entities and aggregates; the owners of derived facts; the repository interfaces (`RecordingRepository`, `SessionRepository`, `AthleteRepository`). Qt objects for signals, no QML | Persistence, device or UI code |
| **Application** | `AppState`; use cases (the commands that change the domain); services that run work: capture, the analysis pipeline, re-analysis, the athlete catch-up, the archive and the report | Holding domain data of its own; writing a file except through an aggregate |
| **Adapters** | Devices (a launch monitor is a device like any other; it simply needs no algorithm); persistence (`SwingStore`, the Recording store, implements `RecordingRepository`; the session and athlete stores implement theirs); UI (presenters, the words module, QML) | Being depended on by an inner ring |

**The Coaching folder.** From Stage B, Part 1 of the platform refactor, `src/Coaching` is the Coaching context's
folder (§2), with no code yet. Its former files, the derived-fact rules (the shot's derived data, M-SHOT-LAYERS layer
3, and the cross-shot reductions), moved to `src/Diagnostics/derived/` in that stage's scripted move, because the findings
rule includes them and R-CTX-1 keeps them out of a later context; the priorities' types are renamed in the same stage (GL-71)[^ctx-24].
Interpreting a result against a golfer's stated intent and goals (the play debrief, decision quality against outcome,
a work-on's verdict, the range-to-course gap) is Coaching's and lives in `src/Coaching`, never in Diagnostics.

### 4.1 Directories, mapped

This is the ring table `src/Gui/tests/architecture_lint_test.cpp` enforces: a directory's ring, with named files
placed elsewhere where a directory spans rings. The **Context** column says which context (§2) each directory serves;
contexts are tagged, not foldered[^ctx-24]. Lint A9 reads the column (§1.4), and a C++ file whose directory has no
context fails it. Every row is placed: the rows once marked "to place in Stage B" were placed by R13[^sa24-r13]. The Vocabulary areas keep the
subject names ruled for Stage B; `Vocabulary/Coaching` is added when coaching types arrive[^ctx-24].

**Names.** "Focus" means two unrelated things and must not be reused for either:

| Id | Today's name | Kind | Target name |
|---|---|---|---|
| NAME-1 | `focusedShotId` | Application state: what is selected | `selection` |
| NAME-2 | `focusConditionId` | Domain source fact: what the golfer declared they are working on | part of `sessionIntent` |
