## 5. Domain objects

Every first-class domain object is C++; QML holds no domain objects or rules[^dec-01].

### 5.1 The tree

```
Athlete  (aggregate root · AthleteId)
 │  record: name, handedness, height, sex/dob, bag, …          ← source fact, athlete.json
 │  folder: where athlete.json is found; no folder key (DEC-151)
 │  tuning log: the golfer's label corrections, in order        ← source fact, outcome_corrections.jsonl
 │  sessions: SessionIndex (SessionId → a typed entry, status included: DEC-136)   ← index only; sessions load on demand
 └─ AthleteDiagnostics      derived, from SESSION summaries

Session  (aggregate root · SessionId, a uuid minted at Start)
 │  identity: SessionIdentity (folder, start time, local day, n-of-day, type)   ← properties, never keys
 │  status: InProgress | Ended, start and end times             ← source, the session file
 │  clubBaseline: computed by the athlete level, frozen here    ← recorded input, club_baseline.json
 │  sessionIntent: declared focus (the declared work-on), declared miss, screen answers, intent runs   ← source fact
 ├─ shots: Shot*  (ShotRef = SessionId + swing number) · each Shot its own object, with its own commands,
 │               reachable only through its Session (P10)
 │    Recording (kind: captured | device-only) · analysis (recorded product)
 │    measurements: one set, each tagged with its source and its kind (reading | derived)
 │    review: reading exclusions · club correction · declared club · shotIntent · rating/note ·
 │            label corrections                                                  ← source facts
 │    effective club                                                              ← derived
│    derived data: outcome · its three factors · carry · flight check (DEC-121) ← derived: computed by the Shot, kept by its Session (DEC-135)
 │    findings: fired by the Shot itself (DEC-100)                                  ← derived: kept by its Session (DEC-135)
│    diagnostics: ShotDiagnostics (read-only view of this shot's findings and results)
 ├─ SessionDiagnostics      keeps the shots' findings and derived data (DEC-135); holds the usuals (DEC-130, DEC-174);
 │                          diagnoses the session from its shots' findings; keeps the diagnosis
 └─ SessionSeries           derived, in memory only
```

### 5.2 Identities

The identity types live in `src/Vocabulary/Identity`, in the Vocabulary ring[^dec-148].

| Id | Identity | Representation | Minted by, when | Persisted | Immutable | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ID-ATHLETE | `AthleteId` | a uuid | `Athlete` creation (today `AthleteController::createAthlete`, `QUuid`) | `athlete.json` (today the settings group `athletes/<uuid>`), and in every swing header (`athlete.uuid`) | yes | The athlete's name and library folder are properties. The Unassigned owner's: [§5.11](#511-the-unassigned-owner)[^dec-198] |
| ID-SESSION | `SessionId` | a uuid[^dec-41]: random for a new session; for a session older than step 2, a name-based (v5) uuid of its athlete's id and its folder name[^dec-150] — "its athlete" being the athlete who OWNS the session's folder (from `athlete.json` once M1 has run, this machine's record before it), the uuid its swings recorded only when no owner can be found[^j-39] | `Athlete::startSession`, at Start[^dec-78]; `Athlete::resumeSession` keeps it[^dec-77][^dec-134]; an old session's is written by the step-2 migration (R-PERS-7) | the session file FILE-SESS-01, in the session's folder, with its status and start/end times | yes | The folder is a property only; its name comes from SET-04, and it never changes during the session's life: a library re-pointed mid-session ends the session and starts a new one[^dec-154]. The v5 id: today `SessionIdentity::legacySessionId` (`src/DomainModel/session_identity.h`, step 2.1), which fixes its namespace and name string (the exact recipe, fixed for ever)[^j-12]. |
| ID-SWING-NUMBER | the swing number | an integer ≥ 1, the `NNNN` of `swing_NNNN` | `Session::addShot`, before capture writes[^dec-142] (today `SwingPaths::allocateSwingDir`) | the folder name; the session's highest issued number in FILE-SESS-10 | yes | Unique within its session and never reissued, even after the shot is trashed; gaps stay (today's rule, kept)[^dec-91]. "Shot N" always means swing number N. There is one allocator: a device-only shot goes into the running session; sessions already split are left as they are[^dec-157]. |
| ID-SHOT | `ShotRef` | (`SessionId`, swing number) | with the shot | n/a: composed from the two above | yes | The only key of a shot outside its own folder. The type: today `src/Vocabulary/Identity/identity.h` (step 2.1), with `AthleteId` and `SessionId` |
| ID-CLUB | a canonical club id | the club vocabulary's id (`club_vocabulary.h`) | n/a: vocabulary | wherever a club is named | yes | Labels come from one table (R-CLUB-5) |
| ID-RIG | the rig state: `RigRef` = (`RigId`, `RigStateId`) | `RigId`: a uuid. `RigStateId`: a digest of the rig state's canonical bytes (the versioned part only; the time the state came into force, `sinceUtc`, is outside it). A calibration record may carry `evidence`, where its raw run is kept; like `sinceUtc` it is outside the digest: setting or changing it makes no new rig state and emits no `RigChanged`[^sa25-add2]. The canonical bytes fix how every number is written, so every machine computes the same digest | `RigId`: by the `Rig`, the first time a machine has none stored. `RigStateId`: computed whenever the versioned part changes. `RigRef`: n/a, composed | `rig.json` (FILE-APP-07: the `RigId` and the current state); each Recording's `rig` block (FILE-SHOT-01: `id`, `stateId`) | yes: `RigStateId` is a function of the content, so putting a camera back as it was gives back the earlier id | The same set-up has the same id wherever and whenever it is computed. An equal `stateId` means the same captured set-up; an unequal one need not mean a different one (R-RIG-1), and no reader checks a block against its `stateId`. A `RigId` that is not this machine's is normal in a Recording. The three types live with the other identity types. See [§2.3](#23-studiorig)[^dec-17][^sa24-rig]. Built: the three types in `identity.h`, and the digest (SHA-256 of the canonical bytes, `rigStateIdOf` in `src/Vocabulary/Rig/rig_state.h`); nothing mints a `RigId` or persists either yet (C-4, C-8b) |
| ID-DEVICE-KEY | `DeviceKey`: one physical device | a camera: its description, serial number and device id; a sensor: its description and device id. Hashable, ordered, with a null | n/a: built from the device's own facts | wherever a device is named, in each map's own spelling (below) | n/a: a value built from the device's own facts, never minted | A value type in `src/Vocabulary/Rig/`. A sensor's key has two persisted spellings, with and without its description, which the type names (`persistedWithDescription()`, `persistedId()`); each map's reader and writer states which it uses. Two sensor keys are equal when their device ids are; the description is carried only for the alias spelling, and `imu/alias` is looked up by that spelling, never by key equality. A camera key is equal when both its parts are[^sa25-add9]. A key that fits no spelling is not a `DeviceKey`: the copy from settings keeps such an entry verbatim and matches it to no device[^sa25-add10]. A phone camera's key (`peer_id` + `source_id`) has room in the type; Stage F builds it[^sa24-rig]. Today open-coded strings; built; C-1b and C-1c put it to use. `#wide`, used by `camera_roi.h` (`camera/exposureUs`, `camera/tuning`), reproduced; §4.6's list omitted it[^sa24-rig] |
| ID-PLACEMENT-KEY | `PlacementKey`: one thing that can hold a role | a `DeviceKey` and an optional lane, the lane spelled as `hm_unit_id.h` spells it. A Witmotion has no lane; a HackMotion has two, lower arm and palm, so one device holds two roles. Hashable, ordered, with a null | n/a: built from a `DeviceKey` and a lane | `imu/roles` (SET-09) and the sensor entries of the rig state and the `rig` block | n/a: a value built from a `DeviceKey` and a lane, never minted | A value type in `src/Vocabulary/Rig/`. In the Rig and in the `rig` block a sensor entry is keyed by its `PlacementKey`, so a HackMotion appears as two entries[^sa24-rig]. Today open-coded strings; built; C-1c puts it to use |
| ID-NORM-SET | a pack or norm set | its file name (today) | n/a: named by whoever writes the file | the file name | yes, while the file exists | The identity this page gave packs and norms at `108e7858`; the owner is `RuleData`[^dec-170]. EVT-12 and EVT-13 carry it. |

**Device keys and their spellings.** Each persisted map keeps today's spelling, byte for byte, so nothing is
migrated[^sa24-rig]. Built by C-1a to C-1c.

| Map or field | Keyed by | Spelling |
|---|---|---|
| `camera/perspective`, `isMirrored`, `fixedInPlace`, `ballRoi`, `alias`; `camera/excluded` (a list) | `DeviceKey` | `description + "\|" + (serial, or the device id when there is none)` |
| `camera/roi` (not a rig fact) | a `DeviceKey` and a perspective | the camera spelling, plus `#impact` for the impact strip (`camera_roi.h`); the type offers this spelling, so `camera_roi.h` stops building it |
| `camera/exposureUs`, `camera/tuning` (not rig facts) | a `DeviceKey` and a role | the camera spelling for the Impact role, plus `#wide` for every other role (`camera_roi.h`). `#wide`, used by `camera_roi.h`, reproduced; §4.6's list omitted it[^sa24-rig] |
| `imu/alias` | `DeviceKey` | `description + "\|" + deviceId` |
| `imu/roles` | `PlacementKey` | the bare `deviceId`, or `deviceId#lowerArm` / `deviceId#palm` |
| `imu/excluded` (a list) | a sensor's `DeviceKey` | the bare `deviceId` |
| The club-length prior, `analysis/clubLenPrior` (SET-05) | (`AthleteId`, club id, the face-on camera's `DeviceKey`) | `athlete uuid + "\|" + club + "\|" + camera spelling` |
| The swing's `source.serial` | unchanged | as written today |
| A phone camera | `peer_id` + `source_id`: the type has room for it; Stage F builds it | n/a: not built in Stage C |

**Foreign ids and their translations.** Nothing outside its own adapter keys on a foreign id[^dec-90].

| Foreign id | Where | Translates to | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| The session folder name (e.g. `2026-06-05_Mark-Liversedge_Swing_01`) | the library; `DeviceOnlyMeta::sessionId`; `trashSession(sessionId)`; `restoreSession(sessionDir)` (a path) | `SessionId` through `SessionRepository` | Today the folder name **is** the key (debt, [§11](#11-where-the-code-is-today)). In the target only `SessionIdentity` and `SwingPaths` read it (P7, lint A3). |
| The swing folder name `swing_NNNN`; `RecordingHeader::Identity{id, index}`; `DeviceOnlyMeta::swingId` / `swingIndex` | the swing folder and header | the swing number of a `ShotRef` | One meaning: `id` is the folder name, `index` the swing number |
| `RecordingRepository`'s `swingDir` key | every repository call today | `ShotRef` | Step 2 re-keys the repository (debt) |
| PPCP's `peerId`, `sessionId`, `captureId`, `streamId` (`StreamOrigin`) | a phone clip's stream origin in the Recording | n/a: provenance only (today: `recording_repository.h`) | Nothing in `src/Analysis` keys on them (today: `recording_repository.h`); a PPCP "session" is a peer session, not a `Session` (§12)[^dec-118] |
| PPCP annotation scope `session_id` / `shot_id` | FILE-SHOT-11 | design: the platform refactor (capture) | design: the platform refactor (capture) |
| The launch monitor's own shot number and session files | the device's reading, kept in the Recording as delivered | n/a: kept as delivered (today) | A device session is not a `Session` (§12)[^dec-118] |

### 5.3 The objects

Role: aggregate root, entity, value, member (a part of the object named), index, view, or application state.
Fact kind: P1's source, recorded input, derived or recorded product.

**What each object is:**

| Id | Object | Role | Fact kind | Owner (root); level | Identity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-ATHLETE | `Athlete` | aggregate root | n/a: holds the facts below | itself; athlete | ID-ATHLETE |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-RECORD | Athlete record: name, handedness, height, weight, sex, dob, handicap, primary club, speed target, notes, the report's details (email, greeting, photo consent and its date), created at; the Wrist chart's reference swing, a `ShotRef`, one per athlete[^dec-152]; the bag (OBJ-BAG) | member of `Athlete` | source | `Athlete`; athlete | the athlete's |
| OBJ-BAG | The bag: the athlete's clubs, each a `Club` record (canonical id, loft, lie, length, shaft, retro-band centres and band width, the taped-on date, hosel, the head patch, where the hands end, a note; today: `athlete_controller.cpp`) | member of the record | source | `Athlete`; athlete | per club: ID-CLUB |
| OBJ-TUNING-LOG | The tuning log: every label correction the golfer made, in order, including ones later overwritten[^dec-43] | member of `Athlete` | source | `Athlete`; athlete | n/a: one per athlete, append-only |
| OBJ-SESSION-INDEX | `SessionIndex`: `SessionId` → a typed entry, status included[^dec-136], with the session's summary[^dec-53]; the entry's step-2 fields: step 2's brief[^dec-160]; the entry's summary, read through `SessionRepository` (`deferredSummary`, not `enumerate`): the swing roll-up (the swing count, the first swing's time, the span, the clubs, the devices, the mean score, the thumbnails, whether every swing is indexed), the size on disk, the lead work-on (work-on 1 of FILE-SESS-05, this build's rule only), and the gate counts (FILE-SESS-05's `gates`, under any rule; absent: not summarised yet)[^sa24-p3b]; a fact not known is NA, never 0[^sa-p1] | index | derived | `Athlete`; athlete | n/a: one per athlete |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-DIAG | `AthleteDiagnostics` | derived owner | derived | `Athlete`; athlete | the athlete's |
| OBJ-SESSION | `Session` | aggregate root | n/a: holds the facts below | itself; session | ID-SESSION |
| OBJ-SESSION-IDENTITY | `SessionIdentity`: folder, start time, local day, n-of-day, type (the session type chosen at Start; read for old files, it decides nothing[^dec-202]) | member (properties) | source | `Session`; session | n/a: properties of ID-SESSION |
| OBJ-SESSION-STATUS | `Session.status`: InProgress / Ended, start and end times | member | source | `Session`; session | n/a: an attribute |
| OBJ-CLUB-BASELINE | Club baseline: the athlete's per-club baselines as this session passes them down to its shots' findings | member | recorded input[^dec-21][^dec-46] | `Session`; session | n/a: one per session |
| OBJ-SESSION-INTENT | `sessionIntent`: declared focus (the declared work-on, GL-68)[^dec-138], declared miss, screen answers, "from here on" intent runs | member | source | `Session`; session | n/a: one per session |
| OBJ-SHOT | `Shot` | entity: its own object (P10) | n/a: holds the facts below | `Session` holds it in its collection; the `Shot` owns and manages its own facts (P10); shot | ID-SHOT |
| OBJ-RECORDING | Recording (§2.2), with its analysis context (OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT, a recorded input) and its rig reference (`RigReference`, a recorded input at the shot level: `Unknown`, `None`, or `Known` with an embedded copy of the rig state it was captured with and its `RigRef`, ID-RIG)[^sa24-rig]; the rig reference is built by C-8a, C-8b | member of `Shot` | source (the context and the rig reference: recorded inputs) | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT | The analysis context (§2.2): handedness, height, club set, the captured club, the bag snapshot | member of the Recording | recorded input | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS | The analysis: stamped with its stage versions and inputs; window time | member of `Shot` | recorded product | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-MEASUREMENT | Measurement `{metric, source instrument + route, kind, value, σ, gates}`, one set per shot | value, in a set the `Shot` holds | per kind[^dec-54]: a **reading** (delivered by an instrument: LM, HackMotion) is source; a **derived measurement** (rules over the Recording's streams: camera, IMU, and the LM's inferred reads) is part of the recorded product | `Shot`; shot | n/a: (shot, metric, source) |
| OBJ-SHOT-REVIEW | The shot's review: reading exclusions, the club correction, the declared club, shot intent, rating, note, label corrections | member of `Shot` | source | `Shot`; shot | n/a: one per shot |
| OBJ-EFFECTIVE-CLUB | Effective club (R-CLUB-2) | value | derived | `Shot`; shot | ID-CLUB |
| OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS | The shot's findings (layer 5 of M-SHOT-LAYERS) | fired by the `Shot`[^dec-100], 1:1 with the shot that fired them[^dec-101]; a member of its `Session`'s collection[^dec-135] | derived | `Session`, which keeps them per shot[^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED | The shot's derived data (layer 3 of M-SHOT-LAYERS): the shot outcome, the shot's tendencies (its three factors: strike · direction · flight), carry against the usual, and the flight-check overlay, computed for the shot with the session's usuals and the athlete's hand (the shot's own recorded hand, else the athlete record's) passed down[^dec-121];[^dec-122];[^j-102] | computed by the `Shot` (P10); a member of its `Session`'s collection, with the shot's findings[^dec-135] | derived | `Session`, which keeps it per shot[^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SHOT-DIAG | `ShotDiagnostics` | view: read-only, of OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS and OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED, exposed through the `Shot` | derived | n/a: a view owns nothing; it reads the findings and derived data its `Session` keeps for the shot[^dec-100][^dec-135]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-SESSION-DIAG | `SessionDiagnostics`: diagnoses the session from its shots' findings[^dec-100]; its diagnosis carries the session's sequence-consistency reading (the order share and the peak-timing spread), worked out once with it, held in memory and never persisted[^sa-j82]; computes and holds the usuals, in the snapshot its shots fire against (GL-25)[^dec-130][^dec-174]; keeps the shots' findings and derived data for its `Session`[^dec-135] | derived owner | derived | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY | Session summary: the one session-level product behind the session report. The report renders it, the athlete level reads it, and nothing in it is computed twice[^dec-81]; it is `work_ons.json`[^dec-59]. Its fields, as the starting list[^dec-92], which step 3 checks field by field against the client report: per shot, its `ShotRef`, effective club, outcome and three factors; per club, the usuals and carry today; the conditions with their tiers, links and reasons; the intent summaries; the session work-ons (GL-69)[^dec-138]; per club, the club baseline window's per-shot values and mishits, from which later sessions' club baselines are computed (the list: step 3's brief, D7)[^dec-172]. Its typed results are value types over today's JSON keys, their field list shown to the Solution Architect before step 3.5 builds it[^dec-173]; it grows by the field list's in-memory types — per shot, outcome and factors; per club, usuals, carry today and the baseline window; the reasons — so the athlete level reads summaries only; older files without them still load; the intent summaries not built[^dec-58][^dec-192]; and, for the same purpose, compact per-shot facts — what the athlete's tendencies, themes and consistency read of each shot, never its judged rows[^dec-192]; and the day readings another session's Charts and report read of it — per shot its delivery readings and its readings at address, and the session's cameras (FILE-SESS-05's `dayReadings`)[^dec-74-sum]; and the gate counts the model browser's Gates view sums over every athlete's index (FILE-SESS-05's `gates`)[^sa24-p3b] | member of `SessionDiagnostics`' results | derived | `SessionDiagnostics`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-SESSION-SERIES | `SessionSeries`: the session's measurements as series, one per session | derived owner, in memory | derived | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-PHASE-GRID | The shot's phase grid (the sampler's numbers per phase) | shot cache | derived | `Shot`, which writes it[^dec-103]; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-MARKUP-TRUTH | The video markup's ground truth (FILE-SHOT-12) | member of `Shot` | source[^dec-83] | `Shot`; shot | the shot's |
| OBJ-REPORT | The session report: its PDF, its metadata and its stills, a rendering of the session summary[^dec-81] | product | derived, except its metadata's coach note and send log, which are source facts[^dec-140], kept in a file of their own from step 5 (FILE-SESS-13)[^dec-201] | `Session`; session | the session's |
| OBJ-PACKS-NORMS | Packs and norms: the shipped core pack and norms; the user's pack, corridor and content edits; community sets; flight norms; the set of disabled norm sets | rule data | source (the user's edits); shipped rule data (read-only) | `RuleData`, one per machine, its type in `DomainModel`; application[^dec-170] | ID-NORM-SET |
| OBJ-RIG | `Rig` (§2.3): the rig state (versioned) and the Rig's other facts (not versioned); the camera calibration design's Bay[^sa24-rig]. The class exists, with its camera commands (C-4, C-5a.1) and its sensor commands, `claimRole` and `releaseRole` (C-6a.2; their contract `RigSensorCommands`, `rig_commands.h`), which keep invariant 2 (one claim per role) by calling `imu_roles::claimRole` over the rig state's roles with the hardware facts handed in as a typed value (`rig::SensorFactsTable`, `Vocabulary/Rig/sensor_facts.h`), and which nothing in the app calls until C-6b.1; built by C-4 to C-6b.2 | entity | source (calibration results and the set-up), the rig state versioned; the club-length prior learned rig state[^dec-153] | itself; application, one per machine[^sa24-rig] | ID-RIG |
| OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE | `StudioProfile`: the studio's and coach's details for the printed report and its email (the studio's name, logo, accent, address, phone, email, website and booking link; the practice steps; the email's subject and body; the coach's name, title, email, phone and photo); an input to OBJ-REPORT, through the report's inputs stamp. It holds no camera, sensor, position or calibration fact[^dec-193] | entity | source (the coach's entries) | itself; application (per machine: each machine holds its own) | n/a: one per machine |
| OBJ-LIBRARY | The library: the root folder every repository uses; when SET-03 is empty, `<AppData>/swings` (today: `swing_paths.cpp`) | application setting | n/a: a place, not a fact | the app; application (per machine) | n/a: SET-03 |
