**How a view reaches it, and what it signals** (views read through `app`, [§9.2](#92-the-qml-boundary)):

| Id | View path | Emits | Consumes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-ATHLETE | `app.athlete` (= `app.state.currentAthlete`) | EVT-14, EVT-17, EVT-20, EVT-23[^dec-134] | EVT-06 (appends the tuning log) |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-RECORD, OBJ-BAG | through `app.athlete` | EVT-14 (with `Athlete`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-TUNING-LOG | n/a: not shown; read by the outcome tuning tools | n/a: none | EVT-06 |
| OBJ-SESSION-INDEX | through `app.athlete`; from step 5 the one session list is a view over it, the Archiving tab over every library `Athlete`'s (R-QML-6)[^dec-200][^j82-archiving] | n/a: no event of its own | every event whose chain reaches the session summary (§7.4); EVT-17…20 and EVT-23 (an entry added, archived, restored, removed, resumed) |
| OBJ-ATHLETE-DIAG | through `app.athlete`; until step 4, `WorkOnsController` as its thin adapter, created in C++[^dec-165] | n/a: change signals only | marked stale by every event whose chain ends at the athlete level (§7.4; R-DER-4) |
| OBJ-SESSION | `app.session` (= `app.state.sessionOnScreen`); until step 4, `app.sessionAt(folder)`, answered by the current `Athlete`, then by whichever library `Athlete`'s folder holds it, the Unassigned owner's included (`Athlete::sessionAt`, the folder translated at the edge)[^dec-90]. A session belongs to its athlete whoever is current (P3, P6), so a late write after a switch of golfer, and a session captured with no golfer, reach their `Session`[^dec-164][^j-67] | EVT-01, 03, 08…10, 16, 18, 19 (EVT-11 removed; EVT-23 is the `Athlete`'s)[^dec-154][^dec-134]; it hears its shots' events (P10) | EVT-12, 13 (through `SessionDiagnostics`) |
| OBJ-SESSION-IDENTITY, OBJ-SESSION-STATUS | through `app.session` | EVT-09 (with `Session`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-CLUB-BASELINE | through `app.session` | EVT-16 (with `Session`) | n/a: frozen; never EVT-14 |
| OBJ-SESSION-INTENT | through `app.session` | EVT-08, 10 (with `Session`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-SHOT | `app.state.selection.shot`, `app.state.castSelection.shot` (each resolved through its session, P10) | EVT-02, 04…07, 21, 22, from its own commands (P10)[^dec-103] | n/a: its own commands change it |
| OBJ-RECORDING | through the shot; played through `app.state.replay` | EVT-22 (with its `Shot`) | n/a: changes only by its late parts (R-REC-2) |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS-CONTEXT | n/a: read by the job builder only | n/a: none | n/a: never EVT-14 (a recorded input) |
| OBJ-ANALYSIS, OBJ-MEASUREMENT | through the shot; as series through `SessionSeries` presenters | EVT-02, 22 (with its `Shot`) | marked stale by every event that changes the effective context: EVT-04, EVT-21, and EVT-22 when follow-club brought a device club (R-ANA-1, R-ANA-5)[^dec-133] |
| OBJ-SHOT-REVIEW | through the shot | EVT-04, 05, 06, 07, 21 (with its `Shot`) | n/a: changed only by command |
| OBJ-EFFECTIVE-CLUB | through the shot | n/a: derived | recomputed on EVT-04, EVT-21, EVT-22 |
| OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS, OBJ-SHOT-DIAG | `app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics` | change signals of its `Shot` | every event whose chain re-fires the shot's findings (§7.4) |
| OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED | `app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics` (factors, carry: §9.2) | change signals of its `Shot` | every event whose chain recomputes the shot's derived data (§7.4) |
| OBJ-SESSION-DIAG, OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY | through `app.session`; until each view moves (steps 4–5), `SessionDiagnosticsModel` as `SessionDiagnostics`' thin adapter, created in C++[^dec-165] | change signals | every event whose chain reaches the session reduction (§7.4) |
| OBJ-SESSION-SERIES | presenters (LM board, Charts, report) over `app.session` | change signals | EVT-01, 02, 03, 05, 22[^dec-109]; EVT-04, 21[^j-74] |
| OBJ-PHASE-GRID | n/a: read by presenters through the session | n/a: none | rebuilt when its stamp is stale |
| OBJ-REPORT | through `app.session` | n/a: none (no consumer)[^dec-70] | n/a: a product |
| OBJ-PACKS-NORMS | the pack and corridor editor and the content editors, through `RuleData`[^dec-170] | EVT-12, 13 (`RuleData`, publishing a new snapshot)[^dec-170] | n/a: changed only by `RuleData`'s commands, which the editors call[^dec-170] |
| OBJ-RIG | `app.rig` (its models: cameras, sensors, the derived placement view); a past shot's rig through the shot[^sa24-rig] (built by C-4, C-5b, C-6b.1) | EVT-15[^sa24-rig] (built by C-5a.1); EVT-24, `RigDeviceLabelChanged`, for an alias or an enabled flag[^sa25-add8] (built by C-5a.1) | n/a: changed only by its own commands, which `app.rig`, the devices' adapters and the capture service call[^sa24-rig] |
| OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE | through `app` (`app.studioProfile`); the report reads it by injection[^dec-193][^sa26-afternoon] | n/a: no event; a change shows in the report's inputs stamp, so a session's report reads as stale | n/a: changed only by its commands |
| OBJ-LIBRARY | settings | n/a: none; re-pointed mid-session, the session ends and a new one starts[^dec-154] | n/a: a setting |

### 5.4 Lifecycles

The lifecycles are ruled in [`lifecycle_model.md`](lifecycle_model.md), the lifecycle model, which the Solution Architect owns[^sa25-life]. Each id keeps a row here as its pointer, so every reference to it still resolves.

| Id | Object | States | Commands between them (event) |
|---|---|---|---|
| LC-ATHLETE | `Athlete` | Ruled in [`lifecycle_model.md` §4](lifecycle_model.md#4-an-athlete)[^sa25-life] | n/a: the commands and events are listed there |
| LC-SESSION | `Session` | Ruled in [`lifecycle_model.md` §2](lifecycle_model.md#2-a-session)[^sa25-life] | n/a: the commands and events are listed there |
| LC-SHOT | `Shot` | Ruled in [`lifecycle_model.md` §3](lifecycle_model.md#3-a-shot)[^sa25-life] | n/a: the commands and events are listed there |
| LC-RIG | `Rig` | Ruled in [`lifecycle_model.md` §5](lifecycle_model.md#5-the-rig-devices-and-calibration-records)[^sa25-life] | n/a: the commands and events are listed there |

**Housekeeping**[^dec-80] is the one application service that ends things for good. It has one `purge` command; SET-14 off is documented as a permanent delete of raw frames and shown with a warning[^dec-137];
a saved shot is removed only by the golfer's delete (of the shot, its session or its athlete), and a delete can be undone until housekeeping purges it, even if the OS would empty its own trash first[^dec-175][^sa26-guarantees]. Not built: today a trash goes to the OS trash first (FILE-LIB-01 where the volume has none), and the OS emptying its own trash is a permanent delete outside the app. The library's own trash always, the change DEC-175 filed for after the refactor, is GitHub #193. "Purged" is the end state of
LC-ATHLETE, LC-SESSION and LC-SHOT, and nothing else deletes a source fact permanently, except the archive's raw-frame
drop when SET-14 is off[^dec-137] (and, until the change above is built, the OS emptying its own trash[^dec-175]). Its
settings are SET-10…SET-14. It changes facts only through the roots' commands (P3)[^dec-42]. Today (step 3.8-i)
it is `Housekeeping` (`src/App/housekeeping.*`), reaching the athletes through the composition root's callables (D18 (c))[^dec-167]: its
one `purge` removes the library trash's entries older than SET-10 (or all of them: "Empty now"), carried
out in Stores (`SwingPaths::purgeTrash`); its pass, on the Archiving tab's worker, archives sessions by age (SET-11) or,
oldest first, while free space is below SET-12, into SET-13, keeping raw frames according to SET-14. A session an
`Athlete` holds is archived through that `Session` — the one instance[^dec-190], on its owner thread (P8): `archiveJob`
there, the job (values only) on the worker, the result posted back, EVT-18 (R-CON-3); a folder no `Athlete` holds keeps
the repository's archive in a run of its own (as LC-SESSION's no-golfer path, N1[^n1]; from step 5 a no-golfer
folder is the Unassigned owner's, archived through its `Session`)[^dec-198]. `ArchiveController::runHousekeeping`
and `emptyTrash` forward to it. It also reports the library's size to the settings page[^dec-188].

The archive copies a session to FILE-LIB-02 and verifies every copy byte for byte before it thins any shot to a
stub (FILE-SHOT-10). A restore merges each shot's review three ways, so no edit the golfer made while it was
archived is lost (today: `session_archiver.*`, 108e7858; the rule is R-PERS-8). While a session's archive is out
its `SessionDiagnostics` asks and writes nothing (3.7-a), and a firing read already on a worker writes the swing
caches it rebuilt (the phase grid, a JSON-era summary) only under the session's lock and only when the session has no
archive marker, so no cache lands in a swing the archive is thinning (step 3.8-ii, REVIEW-e R1-m6: the reader's
write, `SwingStoreFiringReader`; no interface changed).

### 5.5 What each class is for

Enough to write its header; anything an implementer would still have to choose is named as an open question. The
command names in this table and the Producer column of §7.4 are ratified as names[^dec-94], and
step 3 may rename them with approval. They are split: the shot-local commands are the `Shot`'s, the session-wide
ones the `Session`'s, the athlete's the `Athlete`'s. `applyAnalysis` and `completeCapture` are the `Shot`'s[^dec-103].

| Id | Class | Responsibilities | Invariants | Commands (event) | Never |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CLS-ATHLETE | `Athlete` | Holds the record, the bag, the tuning log and the `SessionIndex`; owns `AthleteDiagnostics`; starts and resumes its sessions[^dec-78][^dec-134], and makes and holds each `Session` it loads, one per `SessionId`, as its child[^dec-190]. Its step-2 form (record, bag, index, `remove()`, `recordChanged` as the interim for EVT-14) is step 2's brief's[^dec-160] | One instance per `AthleteId` (the identity map); its folder is where its `athlete.json` is found, with no `folder` key, kept on rename, never derived from the name[^dec-04][^dec-151]; at most one of its sessions is InProgress, which `startSession` and `resumeSession` enforce[^dec-78][^dec-134]; the record holds source facts only, so today's `lastSessionAt` and `sessionCount` are derived from the `SessionIndex`[^dec-89] | `create`; `editRecord`, `editBag` (EVT-14); `startSession` (EVT-17)[^dec-78]; `resumeSession` (EVT-23)[^dec-77][^dec-134]; `trashSession` (EVT-20)[^dec-71]; `sessionAt(folder)`, by which a view reaches a `Session` until step 4[^dec-164]; `appendTuningLog` on EVT-06[^dec-43]; `delete` (no event: Q-16)[^dec-79] | Read shot findings; change a session's recorded inputs (P1); be re-pointed at another athlete (a switch replaces it, R-APP-1) |
| CLS-SESSION | `Session` | Holds its collection of shots (adding and trashing them, swing numbers, order), each shot's findings and derived data as the `Shot` posts them[^dec-135], the context they were taken in (identity, type, status, intent run, session intent, club baseline), its derived owners and its session-wide results; hears every change to a shot through the shot's events (P10); asks each shot to re-fire its findings on a corridor or norm edit[^dec-100] | Swing numbers unique, increasing, never reissued[^dec-91]; its status is persisted[^dec-41], so a crash leaves it InProgress until `end` or the athlete's next `startSession`[^dec-73] | `end` (EVT-09; on an empty session it asks `Athlete::trashSession`, EVT-20)[^dec-143]; `addShot` (EVT-01, emitted when capture's document write lands)[^dec-168][^dec-142]; `trashShot` (EVT-03); `setIntentRun` (EVT-08); `setSessionIntent` (EVT-10); `freezeClubBaseline` (EVT-16 on a rewrite); `archive` (EVT-18), `restore` (EVT-19), `saveReport`[^dec-140] (the report command)[^dec-22][^dec-94], which writes the report's derived file (FILE-SESS-07), the PDF (FILE-SESS-08) and the stills (FILE-SESS-09), never its source file, and the commands for the coach's note and a send, which write that source file (FILE-SESS-13)[^dec-201][^proposed-names]. `applyAnalysis` and `completeCapture` are the `Shot`'s[^dec-103]; resuming is the `Athlete`'s `resumeSession`[^dec-134]; `relocate` is removed[^dec-154] | Own, write or command a shot-specific fact (P10), except keeping the findings and derived data its shots post[^dec-135]; read another session's facts except a typed input (its baseline); write an athlete file; fire a shot's findings itself[^dec-100] |
| CLS-SHOT | `Shot` | Its own object (P10)[^dec-95]: holds its Recording, analysis, measurements and review, and manages them with its own commands; fires its own findings from its analysis and the context its `Session` passes down, and exposes them through `ShotDiagnostics`[^dec-100]; it posts its findings and derived data, stamped, to its `Session`, which keeps them[^dec-135]. It is a QObject, a child of its `Session`, exposed to QML directly and typed (P9), and reads the snapshot it fires against (club baseline, pack, norms) from its parent[^dec-141]. A re-fire on a worker reads that snapshot as an immutable copy and posts its results to the GUI thread (R-CON-2, R-CON-3) as step 3's brief §4.4 sets out: the `Shot` holds its latest ask number, and a result is kept only when its number is exactly that[^dec-191] | Obtainable only from its `Session`; its kind (captured or device-only) never changes; every change is saved through the repository (one writer), evented in command order, and propagated (re-firing findings, the summary)[^dec-95]. The `Shot` saves and emits its own changes; its `Session` hears them and propagates them[^dec-103] | `rate`, `note` (an event, proposed with its design; not built: today neither emits one)[^dec-70][^sa26-guarantees]; `correctClub` (EVT-04); `excludeReading` (EVT-05); `correctLabel` (EVT-06); `setShotIntent` (EVT-07); `setDeclaredClub` (EVT-21)[^dec-94]; `fireFindings`, which its `Session` calls[^dec-100]; `applyAnalysis` (EVT-02) and `completeCapture` (EVT-22)[^dec-103]; the markup truth's command, which emits no event[^dec-187] | Be reached except through its `Session`; be keyed by folder; diagnose its session (that is `SessionDiagnostics`')[^dec-100] |
| CLS-RECORDING | Recording | The contract (§2.2) | Written once by capture, in stages (closed list R-REC-2), then by its `Shot`[^dec-86]; the analysis context never changes after capture except by an approved one-off migration (R-REC-1); follow-club stores its device club beside the captured club and never overwrites it[^dec-133][^built-6-2] | n/a: capture writes it | Carry anything Diagnostics may not know (§2.2) |
| CLS-SESSION-DIAG | `SessionDiagnostics` | Diagnoses the session from its shots' findings: tiers, patterns, links, reasons, per-club mixes and the summary[^dec-100], and the session's sequence-consistency reading, worked out once with the diagnosis, in memory only (`SessionDiagnosis::sequence`)[^sa-j82]; computes and holds the session's usuals (GL-25)[^dec-130], in the snapshot below[^dec-174]; keeps, for its `Session`, each shot's findings and derived data as the shots post them, stamped, in FILE-SESS-03[^dec-135]; runs the session reduction; keeps the session summary[^dec-81]; holds, as an immutable snapshot, the pack, the norms and the session's club baseline it passes down to the shots' findings[^dec-110], with the usuals[^dec-174] and the athlete's norm cohort on each shot's date[^dec-171]. A change to a club's usuals recomputes that club's shots' derived data only, never their findings; a shot whose read failed is kept as it was and marked *unread* in FILE-SESS-03, re-asked at the next load[^dec-191]. Its memory cost when every loaded session always runs in a sweep is OPEN (Q-18, step 3) | One per session; always runs[^dec-02]; results stamped (R-STAMP-1) | n/a: driven by events; results posted to it (R-CON-3) | Write a shot's `review` or Recording; fire a shot's findings (the `Shot` does)[^dec-100]; read another session[^dec-110]; build golfer-facing sentences (R-DER-9) |
| CLS-SHOT-DIAG | `ShotDiagnostics` | A read-only view of one shot's findings and results, reached through the `Shot` | Never outlives its session; before its findings are fired it has no value (shown "-"), never a zero[^dec-96] | n/a: read-only | Own or cache anything |
| CLS-ATHLETE-DIAG | `AthleteDiagnostics` | Runs the athlete reductions over session summaries: tendencies, athlete work-ons (GL-70)[^dec-138], themes, baselines (including each session's club baseline before the `Session` freezes it)[^dec-46], distances, consistency, intent consistency | Derived on demand: an event marks it stale and it recomputes when next read[^dec-60]. It reads session summaries through `SessionRepository` (the `SessionIndex` holds them), never shots. For the scoped views (a club scope, a shot filter) it reads each session's judged rows through that session's `SessionDiagnostics`[^dec-195] | n/a: driven by events; the athlete catch-up (an application service) asks it to recompute and persist its caches (04 step 3: the catch-up is cut over "via `AthleteDiagnostics`") | Append the tuning log (that is the `Athlete`'s)[^dec-43]; rerun a session's reduction; read shot findings (except the scoped views' judged rows, through the session's `SessionDiagnostics`)[^dec-195] |
| CLS-SESSION-SERIES | `SessionSeries` | The session's measurements as series, one per session; the presenters filter it by source. A series is metric × source × shot[^dec-109] | Recomputed when a shot is added, re-analysed, trashed or completed, or a reading is excluded[^dec-109] | n/a: driven by events | Keep per-club statistics of its own (R-INS-4) |
| CLS-REPOSITORIES | Repositories | `RecordingRepository` (a shot's files in its swing folder; at `108e7858` this page already put the phase grid and the JSON-era files under it), `SessionRepository` (a session's files; an identity map of the sessions' records, the `Session` objects themselves held by their `Athlete`)[^dec-190], `AthleteRepository` (an athlete's files; an identity map)[^dec-56]. Each: the one door to its files (R-PERS-1), schema checked on read (R-PERS-3), atomic writes (R-PERS-2), the advisory locks for its folders (R-PERS-6) | Keyed by `ShotRef`, `SessionId`, `AthleteId` (today `RecordingRepository` is keyed by `swingDir`: debt); a newer schema (for a derived file, a newer schema or rule version)[^dec-197] is read-only and every write refuses it; a held lock or failed write is reported to the caller and surfaced in the app, never only logged[^dec-140][^dec-126] | Load, create, enumerate an athlete's sessions, the writes each file needs[^dec-111]; `relocate` removed[^dec-154] | Be called by a view or a rule; spell another repository's file names |
| CLS-CLUB | Club and bag | `Club` (OBJ-BAG's entries and OBJ-EFFECTIVE-CLUB): canonical id plus geometry (length, shaft type, retro-band centres, hosel from butt, shaft length, where the hands end: today's `ClubGeometry`, `src/Vocabulary/Recording/analysis_context.h`); the bag snapshot in the Recording is that geometry for every club in the bag | The club set (men / women / junior) is computed from the record: junior under 18 on the shot's date, else women's for "female", else men's (today: `athlete_controller.cpp`); standard lengths come from the club set and height (`club_standards.h`); labels from one table | `editBag` on the `Athlete` (EVT-14) | Default an undeclared club to DRIVER (R-CLUB-4) |
| CLS-PACKS-NORMS | Packs and norms | The shipped and user rule data findings are fired against; one immutable snapshot per change (R-CON-4); a norm's standing (provisional or decided, by whom, when) is a typed field[^dec-127]: typed keys in the norm files, with a schema version[^dec-186]. Owned by `RuleData`, one per machine, application level, its type in `DomainModel`; `RuleDataRepository` is its one door[^dec-170]. The characteristic pack's and the norms' types, and the pack file's shape, are in `src/Diagnostics/model`; the other packs' types, and every loader, validator and registry, in `src/Diagnostics/content` (§2, "The packs")[^sa26-r8] | Every findings stamp carries the pack's and norms' versions and content, the disabled sets (SET-06) and the grade policy (SET-15) in force when the findings fired, because they may change later (R-STAMP-1)[^dec-140] | `RuleData`'s commands, which the pack, corridor and content editors call; publishing a new snapshot emits EVT-12 or EVT-13[^dec-170] | Be re-parsed privately; be first built on a worker |
| CLS-RIG | `Rig` | §2.3: one per machine, application level, in two parts (the versioned rig state; the other facts); the one writer of both and of FILE-APP-07, through `RigRepository`; hands devices the current rig state and, for their own devices, the alias and the enabled flag, each as a typed value, tells them of every change to either[^sa25-add8], and takes back a calibration record or a clear[^sa24-rig]. It keeps R-RIG-1…5; the state in force at launch is the one loaded, which holds no sensor alignment. The class exists (`src/DomainModel/rig.*`), loaded once by the composition root, with its camera commands (C-5a.1); built by C-4 to C-6b.2 | Impact is exclusive; one claim per role (LC-RIG)[^sa24-rig] | §2.3's commands; EVT-15 from each command that changes the versioned part, after the save; EVT-24 (`RigDeviceLabelChanged`) from a change to an alias or an enabled flag, after the save; none for the learned prior[^sa24-rig][^sa25-add8] | Ask a device (its hardware facts are handed in); include `app_settings.h` (the copies from settings are made in its store); save a sensor alignment or restore one at launch; refuse a command because the disk failed; hold a rig fact anywhere but FILE-APP-07 and the Recordings' `rig` blocks (§2.3)[^dec-140][^sa24-rig] |

### 5.6 "Live", "open" and "on screen" are not objects

There is one kind of object, `Session`, and three different facts about it[^dec-11]:

| Id | Fact | Kind | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|---|
| LIVE-1 | In progress or ended | Domain attribute, persisted, true without a screen | `Session.status` (FILE-SESS-01). "The athlete's live session" is a query: their session with status InProgress. At most one InProgress session per athlete (a domain rule). |
| LIVE-2 | Loaded in memory | Identity map | One `Session` instance per id while it is held: the panel, a catch-up, the report and a sweep get the same `Session`. Its `Athlete` makes and holds it, as its child (athlete → session → shot), and `SessionRepository` holds its record; releasing a session nobody is using is step 3.5's (Q-18)[^dec-190]. `AthleteRepository` is one too[^dec-56]. |
| LIVE-3 | On screen; being captured into | Application state | `AppState` ([§6](#6-application-state)) |

### 5.7 Instruments

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-INS-1 | The launch monitor is one device among the others[^dec-15]. | A separate LM model or LM-only store |
| R-INS-2 | A shot has **one** set of measurements, each tagged with its source and its kind (reading or derived). P1 applies per kind. Readings are written by capture (R-REC-2); derived measurements with the analysis. | Parallel per-instrument tables of the same metric |
| R-INS-3 | A metric a device reports is taken from the highest-ranked source that reports it, by one ranking rule[^sa-r-ins-5]. The metric catalogue keeps a launch-monitor reading on its own `lm.*` key beside our estimate, never as a higher rung of the same key; the one ranking rule (`measurementLadder`) alone puts the launch monitor first for whatever judges or shows the value[^dec-203]. | Precedence stated in a presenter or per surface |
| R-INS-4 | The LM board, Charts and the report are presenters over the session's measurements (`SessionSeries`), filtered by source. | A presenter keeping its own per-club statistics |
| R-INS-5 | A metric no device reports is derived by one estimator per metric. It uses every available input through a stated measurement model, gives a value with σ, widens σ as inputs drop out, admits a biased input only once calibrated, and flags with ⚠ any inputs that disagree beyond their σ[^sa-r-ins-5]. | Picking one input as the winner for a derived metric; averaging inputs with no stated model; letting a biased input in uncalibrated; hiding a disagreement between inputs |

### 5.8 Club

Five terms, one meaning each:

| Term | Is | Stored |
|---|---|---|
| **Captured club** | The club capture recorded: the session's club at the shot, with its geometry from the bag, its length prior and its standard length. Empty = undeclared. A source fact, never overwritten[^dec-133]. | The Recording's `analysisContext.club` (and `capture.club`, provenance) |
| **Device club** | The club the launch monitor named on a late reading, which follow-club (SET-08) stores beside the captured club as its own source fact; when follow-club is on, the effective-club rule prefers it to the captured club[^dec-133]. | `capture.deviceClub`, beside the captured club, in `capture.club`'s shape[^dec-133][^built-6-2] |
| **Declared club** | The club named on the shot's swing-edit popover, `review.club`. Until step 6 capture also seeded it (the session club, else the athlete's primary club, else the `DRIVER` stub), and the rule took a seed as a golfer's pick[^dec-31]; follow-club moved a seeded one[^dec-82]. Since step 6 capture seeds none and follow-club never touches it; a stored seed is read as saved[^built-6-2]. | `review.club` |
| **Club correction** | The golfer's statement of the club really hit. Until step 6, only the LM board's override[^dec-31]. | `review.clubOverride` |
| **Effective club** | The club every consumer uses, derived from the four above (R-CLUB-2). Never stored. | n/a: derived |

| Id | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| R-CLUB-1 | Source facts | The captured club (kept in the Recording, never overwritten), the device club follow-club stores beside it, the declared club and the correction (in the shot's `review`)[^dec-133] |
| R-CLUB-2 | The one club, from step 6 | The correction wins, else the declared club. It is the one club for findings, every presenter and re-analysis[^dec-18]. |
| R-CLUB-3 | **Retired at step 6: R-CLUB-2 is the rule**[^built-6-2]. Until then, field by field (`effectiveContext()` v1)[^dec-26][^dec-31] | **With a correction** (one that names another club than the captured one): name = the correction; geometry = the bag snapshot's entry for it, when present; standard length = for the correction, the recorded club set and height; length prior = none. **Without one**: name = the captured club; geometry and length prior = the captured club's; standard length = the declared club's when declared (the popover's club keeps setting the standard length, as today)[^popover-club], else the captured club's. The board's evidence suggestion is never a correction. When follow-club is on, the device club takes the captured club's place in this rule[^dec-133]; today it does so because follow-club overwrites the captured club, the interim. **Known interim**[^dec-72]: when only the popover names a club, the analysis uses the captured club's name while findings and presenters use the declared club (R-CLUB-2); accepted until step 6 removes the seed. |
| R-CLUB-4 | Undeclared | An undeclared club is *undeclared* and its findings are fired as "club assumed", never a hidden DRIVER[^j106-legacy-stub] |
| R-CLUB-5 | Label | From one table |

### 5.9 Commands go through the root

| Step | Who |
|---|---|
| 1. A use case calls a command on the root, or on a `Shot` reached through its `Session` for that shot's own facts ([§5.5](#55-what-each-class-is-for); P10), e.g. `Shot::correctLabel(…)`, `Shot::excludeReading()`; the archive and the report are `Session` commands[^dec-22]. 03's examples, `Session::correctLabel(shotRef, …)`, are replaced by P10 | Application |
| 2. The object that owns the fact validates the change | `Session` / `Athlete` / `Shot` (P10) |
| 3. It is persisted through the repository | `Session` / `Athlete`; for a shot's facts, the `Shot`[^dec-103] |
| 4. A domain event is emitted ([§7.4](#74-events-and-what-they-recompute)), and the `Session` hears its shots' events | as step 3 |
| 5. The derived owners update | `SessionDiagnostics`, `AthleteDiagnostics`, `SessionSeries` |

R-CMD-1: views never write files or mutate findings. (The command was called `excludeShot` before v1.0; it excludes
one reading, not the shot: see "reading exclusion", [§12](#12-glossary).)

### 5.10 Headless use

| Id | Case | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| R-HL-1 | Within the app | Every consumer receives the instance already loaded (the identity maps; a `Session` from its `Athlete`)[^dec-190]. |
| R-HL-2 | Sweeps and tools (catch-up, `regrade_ledger`, swinglab) | Get their `Session` and `Athlete` from the same repositories, headless, on the tool's main thread. A domain object is the same whether a screen shows it or a sweep runs it. In the app, the application services (housekeeping, the catch-up) get them through callables the composition root hands them over those repositories, and include no repository themselves; it adds no domain object (IM-S3-11 not needed)[^dec-167] |
| R-HL-3 | Separate processes (the CLI tools) | Advisory lock files (R-PERS-6). |

### 5.11 The Unassigned owner

Capture with no golfer selected goes to an **Unassigned** owner[^dec-198]: an `Athlete` whose sessions are full `Session`s,
with the same commands, events, files and derived owners as any other (LC-SESSION, CLS-SESSION). Capture is not
refused for want of a golfer. This replaces N1[^n1], under which a no-golfer session ran on
today's path with no `Session` (LC-SESSION).

| Id | Fact | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| R-UNASSIGNED-1 | Identity | An `AthleteId` (ID-ATHLETE) like any other: one fixed, well-known uuid, the same in every library and on every machine, named once in `src/Vocabulary/Identity` as the Unassigned owner's id[^j-66]. Never identified by its name or its folder (P7). |
| R-UNASSIGNED-2 | Folder | `<library>/(unassigned)/`, a name `SwingPaths::sanitise` can never produce: it keeps only `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ -` and turns every other character into `-`, so no golfer's folder can hold a parenthesis. Not `unknown`, today's no-golfer folder, which `sanitise` also returns for a golfer's name with no ASCII letter or digit, so a golfer's folder could already be called that; nor `_unassigned`, which a golfer named so would produce. It is not hidden (a leading `.`), since the athlete listing skips hidden folders. Its `athlete.json` (FILE-ATH-01) holds only its id and the name "Unassigned", so it is found like any athlete's folder[^dec-151][^j-66]. A no-golfer session already under `unknown/` is held by Unassigned (R-UNASSIGNED-4); it is held where it is, read through the repository, never moved: moving library folders is a write nothing needs[^j-66]. The athlete.json is written at the first write (a capture's Start, a move); until then, with a no-golfer session in `unknown/`, the owner is held in memory, since a read never writes[^j-67]. |
| R-UNASSIGNED-3 | The athlete picker | Shown only while its `SessionIndex` holds a session[^dec-198][^q5-2a]. When its last session is moved out while it is the athlete on screen, the app goes to the golfer the session went to, so the empty owner leaves the picker (step 5.1 review B3)[^j-68]. |
| R-UNASSIGNED-4 | A no-golfer folder found on disk | Held by Unassigned, as any of its sessions, and can be moved to a golfer. It keeps its `SessionId` (ID-SESSION is immutable). A session in progress is not moved (P3: never change the athlete under a running session). The move is `Athlete::moveSessionTo` (and `SessionRepository::moveToAthlete`): the session's folder is renamed into the golfer's folder, keeping its name and `SessionId`; only `session.json`'s `athleteId` is rewritten; the swing headers are not (R-REC-1: a capture into Unassigned records no golfer, as with none chosen). Refused while in progress, archived, locked or read-only. Its `Session` is released first, and the destination `Athlete` loads it afresh; the derived files go stale by stamp (the golfer's cohort) and a baseline is frozen at first need. Events: `sessionMovedOut` on Unassigned, `sessionMovedIn` on the golfer; both `AthleteDiagnostics` mark their index stale (step 5.1)[^j-67]. |
| R-UNASSIGNED-5 | How it differs from a golfer | **No norm cohort:** its record has no sex or date of birth, so its shots fire with none (the cohort is read from the record)[^dec-171]. **No club baselines:** its sessions freeze no club baseline (OBJ-CLUB-BASELINE): Unassigned is not one golfer, and pooling different golfers' shots into one baseline is the fault 3.10's review found[^j-59][^j-66]. **No athlete-level figures:** Home shows no tendencies, themes, distances or work-ons for it, for the same reason; its sessions list, open and play, and a notice says why[^j-67]. Nor does its session report compare it: no athlete level (no Tendencies), no same-day siblings (no "Earlier today", no "n of m"), each place saying why in words (step 5.1 review B1)[^j-67-7]. |

---

## 6. Application state

Application state is what the app is doing now. It is not domain, and it is never persisted as domain data
(remembering the last screen or selection, if wanted, is an app preference). Selection is application state, and
`AppState` (OBJ-APPSTATE) lives in `src/App`[^dec-11].

```
AppState  (one per app, src/App)
 ├─ currentAthlete : Athlete          replaced on switch; a switch is refused while capture runs
 ├─ captureTarget : SessionId?        owned by the capture service; exposed here read-only
 ├─ sessionOnScreen : Session         the reviewed session, else the capture target
 ├─ mode : Capture | Replay | Analyse
 ├─ selection : ShotSelection         a ShotRef (or none), scoped to sessionOnScreen
 ├─ replay : ReplayState              what is playing, of the selected shot
 ├─ castSelection : ShotSelection?    while capturing, the capture target's newest shot; with nothing
 │                                    being captured, a shot of the session on screen (which one: Q-07)
 ├─ outcome : ShotSelection           the shot whose outcome the carousel's summary shows: the selection's
 │                                    on every pick, move or clear; the newest shot the session on screen
 │                                    adds (EVT-01), even over an older selection (DEC-196)
 ├─ shotSaving : bool                 read-only: a shot is between its impact and its saved file; computed by the
 │                                    shot processor (the service capturing and saving it), shown here (the Session owns its shots)
 ├─ rearmCapture()                    the toolbar's Capture after a Stop: Capture mode, then the cameras
 └─ backToSession()                   Home's Start or Practise while a session runs: Capture mode; does nothing,
                                      and says so, when none runs
```

| Id | Object | Is |
|---|---|---|
| OBJ-APPSTATE | `AppState` | The members above; one per app; on the owner thread (R-CON-1) |
| OBJ-SHOT-SELECTION | `ShotSelection` | A `ShotRef` or none, resolved through its session; emits a change signal; clears itself when its session closes or is replaced |
| OBJ-REPLAY-STATE | `ReplayState` | What is playing of the selected shot, not a second selection[^dec-11]; its members are left to step 4's brief[^rat-11] |
| OBJ-CREDENTIAL-STORE | the credential store (`Secrets/secret_store`) | One per app: a service and an account in, a secret out, with write, read, has and remove; the report-sending password its first client (`Secrets/mail_credentials`), the API keys not yet. It holds each secret in memory after its first read (or a write) for the rest of the run, so a request never waits on the system's store; an empty or failed read is not held. The one writer of FILE-APP-08[^sa25-k1] |

| Id | Member | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-APP-1 | `currentAthlete` | Replaced on switch; an edit to the record is not a switch. A switch is refused while capture runs (an application rule). | Re-pointing app-lifetime objects at a new athlete by hand |
| R-APP-2 | `captureTarget` | Owned by the capture service; `AppState` exposes it read-only[^dec-57]. That a session is in progress is the session's own attribute; which session capture writes into is application state. | Anything but the capture service setting it; capture writing anywhere but the session it asks |
| R-APP-3 | `sessionOnScreen` | The reviewed session, else the capture target. With nothing captured or reviewed, `AppState`'s own live session: the latest session at launch and on an athlete switch, and today's session after End[^j-a2][^j-69]. | Each view working out "the current session" for itself |
| R-APP-4 | `selection` | Like `QItemSelectionModel` beside an item model: refers to a shot by `ShotRef`, never owns it. Several views share one selection. Resolved through the session, never by folder name. Cleared by signal when its session closes or is replaced. | A view keeping its own selected shot; a selection outliving its session; matching by folder basename |
| R-APP-5 | `castSelection` | A second selection: the wall cast follows the newest shot being captured while the stage replays another. With nothing being captured, it shows the session on screen[^dec-22]; which shot of it, the newest or the selection's, is **OPEN (Q-07, step 4)**. Same rules as `selection`. | The cast keeping a selection of its own outside `AppState` |
| R-APP-6 | `replay` | What is playing, of the selected shot. | A second "selected" shot |
| R-APP-7 | `outcome` | The shot whose outcome the carousel's shot summary shows: set to the selection on every pick, move or clear, and to a new shot when the session on screen emits `ShotAdded` (EVT-01), even over an older selection. A `ShotRef` only (with the folder it was set with), scoped to the session on screen and cleared with it. `ShotReanalysed` refreshes the summary in place and does not move it. When it is not the selection, the summary names its shot[^dec-196]. | The carousel keeping its own "newest shot"; a copy of the shot's facts in it |

### 6.1 What is not domain

| Id | Thing | Layer |
|---|---|---|
| NOTDOM-1 | Current athlete, capture target, session on screen, mode, selections (the outcome's among them: R-APP-7), replay | Application state (`AppState`) |
| NOTDOM-2 | The library root (SET-03), the session naming pattern (SET-04), capture and housekeeping preferences (SET-07, SET-08, SET-10…14, SET-16) | Application settings[^dec-117] |
| NOTDOM-3 | A view's scroll position, open tab, expanded row, chart zoom | View state (the view's own) |
| NOTDOM-4 | A view-local helper: a filter proxy, a chart's metric helper | View state; may be created in QML |
| NOTDOM-5 | Last screen, last selection, the current athlete at launch (SET-02) | App preference |
| NOTDOM-6 | A device's battery band (ok, low, very low): a reading worked out from the device's battery level, and for a phone whether it is charging, by one rule (`src/Vocabulary/Rig/battery_band.h`); each device's owner publishes it beside the level, and the screens only map it to a theme token and its words. Never stored[^sa26-battery] | Application state: a live device reading, derived |

---

## 7. Derived facts

### 7.1 The three levels

| Level | Owner | Inputs (explicit, typed) | Results | Cached in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shot | the `Shot`, layer by layer (M-SHOT-LAYERS)[^dec-121]: its derived data, then the assessment that fires its findings[^dec-100]; it posts both, stamped, to its `Session`, which keeps them[^dec-135]; exposed through `ShotDiagnostics` (read-only) | derived data: the analysis, with the session's usuals passed down as a typed input. Assessment: the analysis and the derived data against the diagnostic graph[^dec-132], each number against its corridor (pack + norms, less the disabled sets SET-06, at the grade policy's thresholds SET-15), with the club baseline, the effective club and the norm cohort on the shot's date[^dec-171] its `Session` passes down | derived data: outcome; the shot's tendencies, strike · direction · flight; carry against the usual; the flight-check overlay[^dec-122]. Findings, as fired | findings and derived data: FILE-SESS-03, kept by the `Session`, as today[^dec-135] |
| Session | `SessionDiagnostics` diagnoses[^dec-100] | its shots' findings and derived data; for the usuals, its shots' readings (GL-25)[^dec-130]; `sessionIntent`; the shots' intent; the session's club baseline | the usuals (GL-25)[^dec-130]; the ledger, the `Session`'s collection of its shots' findings and derived data[^dec-135]; links/tiers, mixes, carry today, reasons, intent summaries (consistency within the session, shot against session intent)[^dec-58], the session summary the athlete level reads | FILE-SESS-03, FILE-SESS-05 (the session summary) |
| Athlete | `AthleteDiagnostics`, derived on demand | session summaries (intent summaries included); the athlete record; for the scoped views, each session's judged rows through its `SessionDiagnostics`[^dec-195] | tendencies, athlete work-ons[^dec-138], themes, baselines (each session's club baseline before it is frozen)[^dec-46], distances, consistency, intent consistency across sessions | FILE-ATH-02 (distances, in `clubDistances`), FILE-ATH-03; baselines are not cached[^dec-172] |

The result names are defined in [§12](#12-glossary).

In the code (step 3.6; status, not a rule): the athlete row's owner exists over the summaries for the athlete work-ons, the
distances, the baselines, the tendencies and the themes; the consistency grades stay `WorkOnsController`'s (their grader is
in the Rules ring since step 3.8-i, `src/Diagnostics/derived/consistency_grade.h`; their window rule is still `WorkOnsController`'s), and
intent consistency is computed nowhere (§11.1's `AthleteDiagnostics` row). Home reads the
owner from the catch-up's cut-over (3.7).

| Id | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| R-DER-1 | Direction of reading | A level reads the level below as *results*; context flowing down (session usuals into a shot, the athlete baseline into a session) is a typed input. |
| R-DER-2 | The analysis pipeline never fires findings | Findings are the `Shot`'s. On a corridor or norm edit the `Session` asks each shot to re-fire, and a club correction re-fires that shot, without re-analysis[^dec-19][^dec-100]; a label correction re-fires that shot[^dec-131]. |
| R-DER-3 | Athlete level | Reads session summaries, never raw shots, and never reruns a session's reduction itself. It computes the club baseline a session then freezes[^dec-46]. Its one exception: for the views under a club scope or a shot filter (the scoped themes and work-ons), it reads a session's judged rows through that session's own `SessionDiagnostics` — the one reader of its `diagnostics.json` — holding the `Session` for the read and releasing it after when it loaded it; never the file, never a store[^dec-195]. |
| R-DER-4 | Athlete level is lazy | Derived on demand and invalidated by events: an event marks it stale, and it recomputes when next read[^dec-60]. The tuning log is appended by the `Athlete` root, not by it[^dec-43]. |
| R-DER-5 | Flight check | Shot derived data (OBJ-SHOT-DERIVED), computed for the shot with the session's usuals passed down, like the outcome; it feeds the assessment that fires the shot's findings[^dec-122]. |
| R-DER-6 | Direction | The pack's corridors everywhere; there is no second direction classifier[^dec-07]. |
| R-DER-7 | Held conditions | One rule: Your swing follows Work-ons[^dec-09]. |
| R-DER-8 | Intent | Never changes a verdict: PPS reports what it saw. |
| R-DER-9 | Words | Results are ids and numbers. Golfer-facing sentences are built in the words module, never in a reduction, and never persisted. |
| R-DER-10 | Chains end at the athlete level | Every event whose chain changes a session summary continues → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112]. |

### 7.2 The analysis is a recorded product

A shot's analysis is derived from its Recording, but it is expensive and produced by versioned stages, so it is
kept in the swing document as a cache with a stamp (`k*StageVersion` and inputs, the effective club among them).

| Id | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| R-ANA-1 | Stale is known | A changed stage, or a club correction, makes the analysis known-stale, visibly. |
| R-ANA-2 | Re-analysis reproduces live | Same Recording + same effective context (the effective club included) → same job. A corrected shot re-analyses with its corrected club[^dec-18]. |
| R-ANA-3 | Produced by | The analysis pipeline (an application service). It produces the recorded product only, and never fires findings. |
| R-ANA-4 | Bumping | A change to a stage's output bumps its `k*StageVersion`. |
| R-ANA-5 | The input stamp | `analysis.versions.inputs` records the effective context the analysis used (the effective club, its length and source, height, club set, handedness[^dec-75]; and the session type[^dec-88]; `kAnalysisInputsVersion`). A shot whose effective context now differs from its stamp, or that has no stamp, is stale. A change of inputs moves no `k*StageVersion`[^dec-25][^dec-36]. |
| R-ANA-6 | One time base | `analysis.timeBase: "window"`: the analysis is window-relative on disk, rebased once in the pipeline; a document without the key is read in one place, the repository's reader[^dec-25]. |
| R-ANA-7 | Reuse | The reuse gate reuses a stage's product only when its stamp matches: the shaft track, the ladder and the DTL club track only when the stamp's club inputs match[^dec-128]. A pose can be reused only if it was saved (SET-16; today). Pose is re-run **only** when its stage version changes, and is reused whatever pose model file the machine has[^dec-64]. A swing with no input stamp (every swing analysed before step 1) counts as analysed with its captured club, so only a corrected club re-tracks its shaft[^dec-144]. A handedness change re-runs the shaft track and the ladder; the reused pose and DTL pose keep their keypoints with lead and trail swapped, never re-inferred, since handedness only labels the wrists[^dec-145]. |
| R-ANA-8 | One ball-track rule | Live and re-analysis use one rule: a valid empty-mat baseline → BallRunner[^dec-35]. |

### 7.3 Stamps

| Id | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| R-STAMP-1 | What a stamp holds | The version of every rule that produced the result, and a fingerprint of its inputs: norms, thresholds (the grade policy, SET-15), the disabled norm sets (SET-06) and the club baseline included. A findings stamp records the norm sets and the grade policy in force when the findings fired, because they may change later[^dec-140]. It records the norm cohort too, from the athlete record on the shot's date[^dec-171] |
| R-STAMP-2 | A stale stamp | The reader recomputes. It neither trusts the cache nor throws it away silently. |
| R-STAMP-3 | Rule versions | Hand-maintained constants declared beside the rule, and checked: a golden or parity test fails when a rule's output changes and its version did not[^dec-61] |
| R-STAMP-4 | A recorded input's rewrite | Only when its producing rule's version is newer (e.g. `kClubBaselineSchemaVersion`, which counts rule changes); the rewrite records the rule version, when, and what it read, and is an event (EVT-16)[^dec-46]. A rule-version bump that would rewrite a recorded input needs the Solution Architect's approval first, proposed with a dry-run count[^dec-84] |

### 7.4 Events and what they recompute

Events `ShotTrashed`, `SessionEnded`, `IntentRunChanged`, `SessionRelocated` and `RigChanged` are decided[^dec-22],
and `SessionRelocated`, EVT-11, is removed[^dec-154]; EVT-16 follows[^dec-46]. Every event is emitted by the object that ran the command (the `Shot` for its own commands, P10[^dec-103]; the `Session`
hears them), on the owner thread, after the change is persisted (§5.9). **Delivery**[^dec-69]: synchronous on the owner
thread, in command order, and an event carries only the id of what changed (P7); a consumer reads anything else
from the owner. EVT-17…EVT-22 and the split of `ShotAnnotated` into EVT-04 and EVT-05 are decided[^dec-70]. A rating or a note gains an event, so that every view of the shot shows the edit; its name, id and what it recomputes are proposed with its design, for the Solution Architect. Not built: today neither emits one[^dec-70][^sa26-guarantees].

| Id | Event | Producer (command) | Carries | Recomputes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EVT-01 | `ShotAdded` | `Session::addShot`[^dec-142], emitted when capture's document write lands, not at allocation[^dec-168] | `ShotRef` | the shot computes its derived data and fires its findings; that club's usuals → every same-club shot recomputes its derived data, not its findings[^dec-130] → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale |
| EVT-02 | `ShotReanalysed` | `Shot::applyAnalysis`[^dec-103] | `ShotRef` | the shot recomputes its derived data[^dec-140] and fires its findings; that club's usuals → every same-club shot recomputes its derived data, not its findings (since a re-analysis can make a shot a part swing and so change its club's pool)[^dec-191][^j-3] → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale |
| EVT-03 | `ShotTrashed` | `Session::trashShot` | `ShotRef` | that club's usuals → every same-club shot recomputes its derived data, not its findings[^dec-130] → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale |
| EVT-04 | `ShotClubCorrected` (was `ShotAnnotated`, a club correction)[^dec-70] | `Shot::correctClub`[^dec-94] | `ShotRef` | marks the shot's analysis stale; **re-fires** that shot's findings with the corrected club, without re-analysis; the usuals of each club whose pool the correction changes (the old club and the new one)[^j-2] → every shot of that club recomputes its derived data, not its findings[^dec-130] → the session reduction; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112]; `SessionSeries` (that shot's series, under the board's club)[^j-74] |
| EVT-05 | `ShotReadingExcluded` (was `ShotAnnotated`, a reading excluded)[^dec-70] | `Shot::excludeReading`[^dec-94] | `ShotRef` | the excluded reading leaves its club's usuals → every same-club shot recomputes its derived data, not its findings[^dec-130] → the session reduction; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112] |
| EVT-06 | `LabelCorrected` | `Shot::correctLabel`[^dec-94] | `ShotRef` | that shot recomputes its derived data with the corrected label and re-fires its findings[^dec-131] → the session reduction; the `Athlete` appends to its tuning log[^dec-43]; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112] |
| EVT-07 | `ShotIntentChanged` | `Shot::setShotIntent`[^dec-94] | `ShotRef` | no verdict: the intent shown on the shot → the session's intent summaries; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112] |
| EVT-08 | `IntentRunChanged` | `Session::setIntentRun` | `SessionId` | pre-fills the intent of shots taken after the change; no shot already taken changes[^dec-20] |
| EVT-09 | `SessionEnded` | `Session::end` | `SessionId` | a status change: the session summary → marks the athlete level stale; an empty session is trashed instead, by `Athlete::trashSession` (EVT-20)[^dec-143] |
| EVT-10 | `SessionIntentChanged` (focus, declared miss) | `Session::setSessionIntent` | `SessionId` | the session reduction only; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112] |
| EVT-11 | `SessionRelocated`: **removed**[^dec-154] | n/a: removed with `Session::relocate`; a library re-pointed mid-session ends the session and starts a new one, same type and club, the declared focus carried (the type read as the coaching modality since 26 Nov 2026: DEC-154) | n/a: removed | n/a: removed; the id is not reused (§1.3) |
| EVT-12 | `NormsChanged` (a norm set saved, a corridor edit among them; SET-06 or SET-15 changed)[^j82-evt] | `RuleData`, publishing a new snapshot[^dec-170] | the set's id | each shot first recomputes its derived data where it reads the changed set (the flight check reads the flight norms, today: `strike_from_flight.h`; P5)[^dec-122]; the `Session` asks each shot to re-fire its findings through the stamp (rule version and input content)[^dec-100], at once, without re-analysis, never "after a relaunch"; then → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112] |
| EVT-13 | `PackChanged` (the characteristic pack or a content set saved: the references, drills, screens or gate words; the flight norms re-read)[^j82-evt] | as EVT-12 | the pack's id | as EVT-12 |
| EVT-14 | `AthleteRecordChanged` | `Athlete::editRecord`, `Athlete::editBag` | `AthleteId` | athlete-level facts; and, when sex or date of birth changed, that athlete's findings: its loaded sessions' shots re-fire at once, the others at their next load through the stamp (R-STAMP-2)[^dec-171]; when the hand changed, its loaded sessions' shots recompute their derived data (not their findings) at once, the others at their next load through the derived stamp[^j-102]; then → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-112]. **Not** a session's club baseline or any Recording's analysis context (recorded inputs) |
| EVT-15 | `RigChanged` | `Rig`, from any command that changes the versioned part: once per command, after the save[^sa24-rig]. Not emitted for an alias, an enabled flag or a learned prior, for a command that leaves the digest unchanged, or for the state loaded at launch. A worn sensor is calibrated at each wearing, so a session usually begins with one or two. Emitted by `Rig`'s camera commands since C-5a.1; nothing listens yet (C-5b) | the new `RigRef` | nothing: no derived fact reads the current Rig. A Recording captured after it references the new state; every earlier Recording keeps its own copy[^sa24-rig] |
| EVT-16 | `ClubBaselineRewritten`[^dec-46][^dec-70] | `Session::freezeClubBaseline`, on a rule-version change | `SessionId` (the rule version and the sessions read are recorded in FILE-SESS-04) | the session's shots re-fire their findings → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-113] |
| EVT-17 | `SessionStarted` | `Athlete::startSession`[^dec-78] | `SessionId` | the `SessionIndex` gains the session; the capture service may set `captureTarget`[^dec-113] |
| EVT-18 | `SessionArchived` | `Session::archive` | `SessionId` | the `SessionIndex` entry shows it archived; nothing re-fires[^dec-113]: the findings stay in the session's FILE-SESS-03, which stays in the library[^dec-135] |
| EVT-19 | `SessionRestored` | `Session::restore` | `SessionId` | the `SessionIndex` entry shows it restored[^dec-113] |
| EVT-20 | `SessionTrashed` | `Athlete::trashSession`[^dec-71] | `SessionId` | the `SessionIndex` loses the session → marks the athlete level stale; a selection or `sessionOnScreen` on it clears (R-APP-4)[^dec-113] |
| EVT-21 | `ShotDeclaredClubChanged` | `Shot::setDeclaredClub`[^dec-94] | `ShotRef` | the declared club sets the standard length, so the analysis' input stamp no longer matches: marks the analysis stale (R-ANA-5); re-fires that shot's findings with the effective club → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale[^dec-113]; `SessionSeries` (that shot's series)[^j-74] |
| EVT-22 | `ShotCaptureCompleted` | `Shot::completeCapture`, on a late part (R-REC-2: a late LM reading, follow-club, a phone clip)[^dec-103] | `ShotRef` | the shot fires its findings; that club's usuals → every same-club shot recomputes its derived data, not its findings[^dec-130] → the session reduction → the session summary → marks the athlete level stale; `SessionSeries`; when follow-club brought a device club, the effective club changes and the analysis is stale (R-ANA-5)[^dec-133][^dec-113] |
| EVT-23 | `SessionResumed`[^dec-98] | `Athlete::resumeSession`[^dec-77][^dec-134] | `SessionId` | the status is InProgress again; the `SessionIndex` entry updates; its later shots follow EVT-01[^dec-113] |
| EVT-24 | `RigDeviceLabelChanged` (a placeholder name)[^sa25-add8] | `Rig`, from `setAlias` or `setEnabledOnThisMachine` (the unversioned part): once per command that changes something, after the save. Not emitted for a learned prior, for a command that changes nothing, or for the state loaded at launch. Built by C-5a.1 (the camera commands); nothing listens yet (C-5b, C-6b.1) | the device's `DeviceKey` and which of the two changed (the alias, or the enabled flag) | nothing: it makes no new rig state. A manager applies only the field that changed to its live devices, and `app.rig`'s lists show it[^sa25-add8] |

`Session::shotChanged` is not a domain event: the `Session` re-emits each of its `Shot`s' events it hears (EVT-02, 04, 05, 06, 07, 21, 22), after it, carrying the `ShotRef`, for one consumer outside the aggregate, the one session list (R-QML-6), which reads that session's row again[^sa-shot-changed].

An athlete created or deleted emits no domain event: **OPEN (Q-16)**.

---

## 8. Persistence

Each file has exactly one writer, inside its aggregate; source facts change only through the root's commands; the
derived owners write their own caches (P3)[^dec-42]. Every file the app writes is listed here
with its writer today and its target owner[^dec-45]; where the design is deferred it says
"design: the platform refactor (capture)" or "design: the platform refactor". A file not in this section is not
the app's to write (C3)[^dec-115]. Where no decision names them, the fact kinds and target owners of
the added files[^dec-45] (FILE-SHOT-03…06, 09, 11; FILE-SESS-07…12; FILE-LIB-01…05; FILE-APP-03, 06) are
ratified[^dec-116]. Until step 3 the session file and the session input are written by today's
callers through `SessionRepository`, and the athlete record by `AthleteController` through `AthleteRepository`:
R-REC-3's interim, extended[^dec-160].

Paths: `<library>` is SET-03; `<athlete>` is `<library>/<athlete folder>`; `<session>` is
`<athlete>/<session folder>`; `<swing>` is `<session>/swing_NNNN`; `<archiveRoot>` is SET-13.

### 8.1 Files

**A shot's files**

| Id | File | Fact kind | Target owner | Writer today | Target writer; repository | Step |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The folder comes from the composition root, with an override for tests and probes (`PINPOINT_RIG_DIR`, which `isolated_stores.h` and `tools/probe/run_headless.sh` set)[^sa24-rig] | source (the rig), application level, per machine[^sa24-rig] | `Rig` (OBJ-RIG)[^sa24-rig] | `Rig`, through `RigStore` (`src/Stores/rig_store`), since C-4: the file and the `RigId`, written at the first launch; since C-5b the camera group, copied at launch, and the cameras' facts, written by the Rig's commands; since C-7 the club-length prior's group, copied at launch, and the learned priors, written by `learnClubLength` (a settings entry whose key does not spell itself again as a `ClubLengthPriorKey` is kept verbatim, as above); since C-7b the cameras' assumed `extrinsics` records (§2.3, "Calibration"), written with each camera's other records by the command that changes its set-up, and by the launch whose load made them again and so changed the state[^sa27-c7b]; the sensors empty in the running app | `Rig`, by its commands, through `RigRepository` (its adapter, the Rig's store, in the stores folder); atomic, the schema checked on read. A file of a newer `pinpoint.rig/N` is read-only to the build: it runs from the fields it knows, or empty, never saves over it, and says so with `ppWarn`, whatever it holds (proposed, for the Solution Architect: that this holds for a newer schema's file whose `RigId` the build cannot read is the implementer's reading of how P3's two sentences overlap, C-5b). A file the build cannot read at all (it cannot be opened, is not JSON, has no `pinpoint.rig` schema line or no valid `RigId`) is renamed `rig.json.unreadable-<UTC time>` and kept, and the run starts as if no file existed: a new `RigId`, the groups copied from settings, saved as normal[^sa27-p]; if the rename fails, the file is read-only as before (proposed, for the Solution Architect: the coordinator's brief and the implementer's call in C-5b, not the ruling). Any entry the build cannot read, wherever it sits (a camera, a calibration record, a sensor, an alias, an enabled flag, a learned club length), is kept exactly as read, outside the Rig's state and its digest, and written back on every save[^sa27-p2]; and a member it does not know, at any level, the fields of an entry it reads included, is written back as it was read[^rig-unknown]. A group is copied from settings in the item that makes the Rig its writer, never deleted from settings in Stage C; when its keys no longer match the fingerprint (an older build changed them), the whole group is copied again and logged[^sa24-rig]. When none of a group's keys exists in settings (the settings reset or deleted, or a later stage removing them), the copy leaves the Rig as it is: no copy and no wipe[^sa27-p]; a club-length setting with a blank or invalid value counts as absent[^sa27-c7]. The copy reads the settings store itself and never writes or deletes a key; a copy at launch is part of the launch's load and emits no event | platform refactor, Stage C: C-4 (the file and the `RigId`); the copy, built switched off in C-5a.2 (the camera group: the composition root names no group yet) and first run by C-5b (the camera keys) and C-6b.1 (the sensor keys); C-7 (the prior); the sensor group's copy (`imu/roles` through `imu_roles::loadRoles` and the `imu/rolesMigrated` marker, `imu/alias`, `imu/excluded`; every `imu/roles` entry the typed keys cannot hold kept verbatim, as above; `imu/calibration` and `imu/mountOrientation` not copied) built, switched off, in C-6a.2 (proposed, for the Solution Architect: the implementer's reading of P1 for this group, C-6a.2: its keys are `imu/alias`, `imu/excluded`, and `imu/roles` once the marker is set or `imu/placement` before it; the marker alone, or `imu/placement` on a machine already moved, is no key) |
| FILE-APP-08 | the system's secure store, per user, per machine: on macOS a generic-password item in the login keychain (service and label the service's name, account the account); on Windows a generic credential in Credential Manager (target `PinPointStudio/<service id>/<account>`, persisted on this machine only); on Linux, which has none every desktop shares, the settings file's `<service id>/password` and `<service id>/passwordAccount`, in plain text, one secret per service. The report-sending password's: `PinPoint Studio report sending`, `PinPointStudio/ReportSending/<username@host>`, `ReportSending/password` | application state (a secret), application level[^sa25-k1] | the credential store (OBJ-CREDENTIAL-STORE)[^sa25-k1] | the credential store (`Secrets/secret_store`), through its platform half (`secret_store_mac.mm`, `_win.cpp`, `_linux.cpp`), for the report-sending password | the credential store, the one writer; its clients call it and never reach the system's store themselves[^sa25-k1] | platform refactor, Stage E: SE-7a (the store, the report-sending password); SE-7b (the API keys) |

### 8.3 Files the app reads or writes that are not domain

Listed so that "the persistence tables are complete" can be checked. Classing them as not domain is
ratified[^dec-114].

| Id | File | What | Why not domain |
|---|---|---|---|
| EXT-01 | Premier's `SessionUploadQueue.*.json`, `SessionLiveSnapshot.*.json`, `ShotReplay/history.json`; GC Quad's `LastShot.csv` | a launch monitor's own files, read by its connector | the device's; the reading lands in the Recording |
| EXT-02 | `imu_log_*.txt`, `hackmotion_log_*.txt` | device logs | diagnostics output |
| EXT-03 | `PinPointStudio_log_*`, `_stats_`, `_analysis_` (`profiler_controller.cpp`, `resource_monitor_controller.cpp`) | the app's own logs | diagnostics output |
| EXT-04 | `pinpointstudio_audio_*.wav` (Desktop) | an audio capture | a tool's output |
| EXT-05 | `<home>/<shot>-<region>.csv` | the data viewer's CSV export | an outbound export |
| EXT-06 | swing zip exports (`swing_zip_exporter`); the corridor editor's CSL export (`model_browser.cpp`) | outbound exports | outbound exports |
| EXT-07 | the Charts stills caches (`posture-picture`, `delivery-picture`; `charts_posture_presenter.cpp`, `charts_delivery_presenter.cpp`) | rendered images, in the cache location | regenerable view caches |
| EXT-08 | the tools' `--out` trees (swinglab, `regrade_ledger`, the parity tools) | run outputs | outside the library |
| EXT-09 | downloaded TTS models; update payloads | tooling | not facts about a golfer |
| EXT-10 | developer dumps behind environment switches: `imu_*.csv` (`PINPOINT_IMU_RECORD_DIR`), `*_frame.png`/`*_mask.png` (`PINPOINT_ADDRESS_MARKS_DUMP`) (`mediapipe_crop_debug.jpg`, once listed here, was written with no switch and is removed)[^crop-debug] | diagnostics output | accepted as not domain[^dec-140] |
| EXT-11 | downloaded LLM and STT models (`llm_controller.cpp`, `stt_processor.cpp`) | tooling | accepted as not domain[^dec-140] |
| EXT-12 | `<session>/.pinpoint-lock`, `<athlete>/.pinpoint-lock` (from step 2) | the advisory lock files (R-PERS-6) | coordination files, not facts[^dec-160] |
| EXT-13 | the app's bundled resources, read by `Gui/launchmonitor/lm_club_art.cpp` (the LM artwork), `Gui/print/pdf_document_writer.cpp` (the sRGB ICC profile), `Gui/swing3d/swing_hull_geometry.cpp` (a mesh) and `Video/bayer_video_item.cpp` (a shader) | shipped resources, read-only | accepted as not domain[^dec-188] |
| EXT-14 | the pose back-ends' model files (`Pose/person_segmenter.cpp`, `Pose/pose_estimator_mediapipe.cpp`, `Pose/pose_estimator_movenet.cpp`, `Pose/pose_estimator_vitpose.cpp`) and their machine probe (`Pose/pose_backend.cpp`, which reads `/proc/driver/nvidia/version`) | tooling and a machine query | accepted as not domain[^dec-188] |

### 8.4 Rules

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-PERS-1 | **One door per file.** Each file has one repository and one format definition. | A file name spelled outside its repository (lint A4); an envelope parsed in more than one place |
| R-PERS-2 | **Atomic writes, one mechanism** (`QSaveFile`, behind one helper, `atomicWrite`, in the Platform ring from step 2)[^dec-160]. The swing file's own writer, libppswing's `writeFileAtomic`, is the store's documented exception: it syncs the file and its folder[^dec-155]. | Remove-then-rename; part-then-rename; a fixed `.tmp`; a second write helper |
| R-PERS-3 | **Schema checked on read.** A newer schema is read-only, and every write refuses it; an older shape is migrated in one step, in the repository. A derived file (a cache or a summary) whose schema **or rule version** is a newer build's — any version it carries: its own, its ledger's, a record's, a block's — is read-only too, never written over, and the session it belongs to is not taken in hand to be summed up again[^dec-197] | A reader handling old shapes itself; an older build writing over a newer build's file |
| R-PERS-4 | **One serialiser per analysis block**, beside its producer: part of the Recording contract. The live detail and the disk form are the same functions, in one time domain. | Hand-kept converters of the analysis block; a live form that differs from the disk form |
| R-PERS-5 | **A cache never holds the only copy** (P1). | Golfer input or a recorded input in `diagnostics.json` or any other cache |
| R-PERS-6 | **Advisory lock files** guard a session or athlete folder against a second process (the CLI tools); the tuning log among them. A lock is short, one per write; a tool refuses a session whose session file says InProgress, unless it is given `--allow-in-progress` for one a crash left InProgress[^dec-158]. | Two processes writing one file unguarded |
| R-PERS-7 | **One-off migrations** of the library run once, with a dry-run report and backups in the shared scratch area, and wait for the Solution Architect. | A silent migration of the live library |
| R-PERS-8 | **Moving source facts** (the archive, the trash) copies and verifies before anything is removed, and never drops what cannot be regenerated by default[^dec-97]. | A move that loses a source fact |

The athlete's folder is where its `athlete.json` is found; it has no `folder` key, is kept on rename, and is never
derived from the athlete's name[^dec-04][^dec-151].

### 8.5 Golfer input has levels

| Id | Input | Level | Stored |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-INPUT-1 | Shot intent | Shot: can differ from the session's ("we lose focus, we react to a bad shot") | the shot's `review` block |
| R-INPUT-2 | A "from here on" intent run | Session: a session statement. It **pre-fills each shot as it is taken**, and each shot can override it. An edited run affects only shots taken after the edit[^dec-20]. A past run is not edited after its session ends, except by resuming the session (today's only)[^dec-134][^dec-159]. | FILE-SESS-02; the pre-filled intent on each shot |
| R-INPUT-3 | Declared focus (the declared work-on)[^dec-138], declared miss, screen answers | Session | FILE-SESS-02 |
| R-INPUT-4 | Intent consistency within a session; shot intent against session intent | Session, derived | `SessionDiagnostics`' intent summaries |
| R-INPUT-5 | Intent consistency across sessions | Athlete, derived from session summaries | `AthleteDiagnostics`' caches |

Intent never excuses a finding: PPS reports what it saw.

---

## 9. Concurrency and the QML boundary

### 9.1 Concurrency

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-CON-1 | Domain objects and `AppState` live on one owner thread: the GUI thread in the app, the main thread in a tool. | A domain object touched from a worker |
| R-CON-2 | Firing findings, the session's diagnosis and re-analysis run on workers over immutable inputs: a shot's analysis, a frozen baseline, a `shared_ptr<const>` pack and norms. | A worker reading a live member |
| R-CON-3 | Results are posted to the owning root, which applies them and emits change signals. Results for a session that has since closed are dropped by identity. | A worker writing a file or a model directly |
| R-CON-4 | Shared rule data (packs, norms, flight norms, the metric catalogue) is loaded once into immutable `shared_ptr<const>` snapshots. A change publishes a new snapshot and EVT-12 / EVT-13. | Lazy singletons first built on a worker; a private re-parse of a pack |
| R-CON-5 | One threading idiom: a single worker pool and one "post result to owner" helper. Built in step 3, with a serial lane per session and the receiver on the GUI thread, for the diagnostics owners[^dec-169]. **One exception, the pose inference thread**[^dec-194]: one long-lived thread, owned by `PoseRunner`, is the only caller of the cached ViTPose session(s); it is stopped before the sessions and the ORT env are torn down at exit, and the tools get it too. How it is built (`src/Pose/inference_thread.h`, its ring, callers waiting on it, the exit points) is in `build/refactor/step-3/3.7/NOTES-P.md`[^proposed]. | A new pool, thread or idiom; a second caller of a cached ViTPose session |

### 9.2 The QML boundary

| Id | Rule | Forbids |
|---|---|---|
| R-QML-1 | QML reaches the app through one object, `app`. `app.state` **is** the `AppState` (current athlete, capture target read-only, session on screen, mode, selection, cast selection, replay, and the summary's `outcome`: R-APP-7)[^dec-196]. `app.athlete` and `app.session` are shorthand for `app.state.currentAthlete` and `app.state.sessionOnScreen`[^dec-63]. Context globals are only devices, settings, logging and `app`. `app` is made in step 3, in `src/App`, with one interim member until step 4, `sessionAt(folder)`[^dec-164]. | A new context property holding athlete- or session-scoped state |
| R-QML-2 | Lists are `QAbstractListModel`s with declared roles; values are `Q_GADGET`s (`ShotFactors`, `Carry`, `Club`, `Measurement`, `Intent`, …). | `QVariantMap`/`QVariantList` shapes documented only in comments (lint A6) |
| R-QML-3 | No domain or session-scoped C++ type is created in QML. View-local helpers (a filter proxy, a chart's metric helper) stay QML-created. | A view owning a session-level model (lint A2) |
| R-QML-4 | QML contains no domain rules. Three exceptions, each of which may shrink and must not grow: the calibration screens (`src/Gui/calibration`), until #291 moves their rules to C++; the Consistency grade words, by the Design Lead's ruling (#219)[^dl-219]; and the frozen files on the list. The check's exception list (`src/Gui/tests/qml_rule_allowlist.txt`, the Solution Architect's) freezes each file the check counts rules in at its count, each line pointing to the issue that moves its rules or clears its line (AD-52)[^sa26-qml-check]. | "Clean vs Solid" in QML; shape by substring; counts parsed from sentences; a threshold or a pass/fail comparison in a QML or JS file (`session_setup_lint_test` W12) |
| R-QML-5 | The carousel (`ShotListModel`, live and review, and the report's copy of it) is a view over `app.session`: its rows are that `Session`'s shots, and it follows the `Session`'s events: a shot added (EVT-01), changed (its `Shot`'s events and change signals, which the `Session` hears: P10) and trashed (EVT-03). It keeps no list, reload or folder read of its own[^dec-199]. | A carousel list built or reloaded from disk; a second list of a session's shots; a row's facts kept after its `Shot` changes |
| R-QML-6 | The Sessions screen, the report list and the Archiving tab show one session list, following the `SessionIndex`'s events: EVT-17…20, EVT-23 and a session summary written[^dec-200]. The Sessions screen and the report list are a view over `app.athlete`'s `SessionIndex` (OBJ-SESSION-INDEX); the Archiving tab stays library-wide, a view over every library `Athlete`'s `SessionIndex`, one group each[^j82-archiving]. A row's fact the index entry does not hold (the report's state, FILE-SESS-07) is read through that `Session` (P11); how: 5.7's brief[^proposed]. | A session list scanned from disk; a list of its own per screen |

**Worked example: the carousel shot summary**, for LM shots:

```
app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics.factors.{strike, direction, flight}
app.state.selection.shot.diagnostics.carry
```

The selection is application state; the shot and its diagnostics are domain. The factors and the carry are the
shot's derived data, kept by its `Session` with its findings in FILE-SESS-03[^dec-121][^dec-135], so the summary is
there whatever panels are on. It updates whenever an event's chain recomputes that shot's derived data (§7.4),
`ShotReanalysed`, `ShotAdded`, a change of its club's usuals[^dec-130] and a label correction[^dec-131] among them.

---

## 10. The ownership questions

Copy this section into every plan or brief that touches data, and fill one row per new or changed thing, citing
the ids it touches. A reviewer answers the same seven questions for the diff.

```markdown
## Ownership

| New or changed thing (ids) | 1. Kind | 2. Owner, level | 3. Created / ended by; identity | 4. If derived: inputs, rule version, invalidating events | 5. Persisted where; the one writer | 6. Ring of each new file | 7. How a view reaches it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| … | source / recorded input / derived / recorded product / application state / view state | … | … | … | … | platform / vocabulary / rules / domain / application / adapter | … |

Information-model change (needs the Solution Architect's approval before it is built)? yes / no, and which of C1–C11:

> An information-model change (IMC, v1.1) is any edit to code, data, a file format or a plan that adds, moves,
> renames, splits, merges or removes any of the following, or changes what this page says about one: (C1) a domain
> object, an owner of facts, or what an aggregate contains; (C2) a kind of fact (a result, a measurement
> family, a golfer input), or the kind of a fact (source, recorded input, derived, recorded product, application
> state, view state) or its level (shot, session, athlete, application);
> (C3) a persisted file, a field family in one (a new block or group of keys, or a schema-version change of an
> existing file), or a settings key that holds a domain fact; (C4) the one writer of a file, or the repository it is
> written through; (C5) an identity: a new key, a new representation, a new translation to a foreign id, or
> identifying an athlete, a session or a shot by anything but its id, or adding an id type; (C6) a dependency
> between levels: a level
> reading anything but the level below as results, or context flowing down that is not a typed input; (C7) a
> domain event, its producer, or what it recomputes; (C8) an object's lifecycle: who creates or ends it, its
> states, or the commands between them; (C9) a ring, or the ring of a directory or file; (C10) anything domain- or
> session-scoped created by a view; (C11) a principle (P1…P11) or a rule (R-*). It is not an information-model
> change to make code match this page, to add
> a metric to the catalogue within an existing measurement family, to bump a rule's or a stage's version when the
> bump only makes caches stale (the stamp already carries it), to update §11, or to edit a glossary entry's wording
> when the edit cites the decision it follows. A version bump that would rewrite
> a recorded input is a change, proposed with a dry-run count of what it would rewrite. When in doubt, it is a
> change.

Does §11 still match the code this touches? yes / updated in the same commit
```

The template's last line is[^dec-105].

| # | Question | Principle |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Is it a source fact, a recorded input, a derived fact, a recorded product, application state or view state? | P1, P2 |
| 2 | Which object owns it, and at which level? | P3, P6 |
| 3 | Who creates and ends it, and what identifies it? | P3, P7 |
| 4 | If derived: what are its inputs, its rule version, and the events that invalidate it? | P5 |
| 5 | Where is it persisted, and by which one writer (inside which aggregate)? | P1, P3, P4 |
| 6 | Which ring does each new file go in? | the rings, [§4](#4-the-rings-and-the-directory-mapping) |
| 7 | How does a view reach it? (`CLAUDE.md`, the Solution Architect's wording) | P2, P9 |

---

| `SessionPriorities`, `SwingThemes` | session and athlete results | OBJ-SESSION-SUMMARY; `AthleteDiagnostics` | 3 |
| `NormPack`, `CharacteristicPack`, `FlightNorms`, the pack and norm providers | rule data | OBJ-PACKS-NORMS | 3; 7 |
| `StudioProfile` (`App/studio_profile.h`) | the studio's and coach's details for the report | OBJ-STUDIO-PROFILE[^dec-193] | platform refactor: built in SE-4. Its keys (SET-20) and its folder (FILE-APP-06) are read and written through its repository, `StudioProfileRepository` (`App/studio_profile_repository.h`), which the composition root gives it over the one store, `StudioProfileStore` (`Stores/studio_profile_store.*`); lint A5 on `studio_profile.cpp` 9 → 0. Its QML API is typed (A6 4 → 0) and QML reaches it as `app.studioProfile`[^se4-built] |

The wrist-pattern identification is an analysis of the shot, and its result is part of the shot's findings (OBJ-SHOT-RESULTS) through the one diagnostic model[^dec-123] | 3 |
